For data that mix several projects and several workflows, four settings are added, with no change for data that do not need them:
  - **`Status Category`**: when the tickets carry it, it decides what is "in progress" (In Progress) and "to do" (To Do); the `in_progress_states` and `todo_states` lists in `flux.yaml` are then used only for tickets without a category.
  - **`portfolio_types` and `excluded_projects`** in `flux.yaml` (the same as for the dependency map), for example `portfolio_types: [Epic, Initiative]` and `excluded_projects: [BUG]` with generic keys: tickets of these types and projects are left out of throughput, lead time, WIP and forecasts. The project is read from the ticket key. The report states how many tickets were set aside, by type and by project.
  - **All the tickets of the database**: the export reads every ticket of every query at each run (no 10,000-ticket batch to merge, no truncation hint), and `compute` reads them all from the database. If the report sets aside no Epic although the database contains some, `statuses` lists the types read and prints the "Écartés au calcul" (set aside at calculation) line: it says what `portfolio_types` recognized.
  - **"Comment lire ce rapport"** (How to read this report): each report ends, before "Limites" (Limits), with a fixed section that defines in short sentences the percentiles, lead time, age, active, waiting and dormant WIP, arrivals and departures, Little's law and the Monte Carlo forecast, with examples computed from the report's own figures. `/monthly-flow` points to it instead of redefining these terms.
  - **`--scope ABC,DEF`** (optional): keeps only the tickets of these projects (keys separated by commas, case-insensitive, or `tous` (all)), read from the ticket key, like `dependencies_jira.py --scope`. Without the option nothing changes: all tickets in the database are read, and `scope_projects` in `flux.yaml` is not consulted (it is used only for dependencies). The filter runs before the exclusions (`portfolio_types`, `excluded_projects`), the dormant tickets and the history. The report starts with "Périmètre : … (N tickets retenus sur M lus)" (Scope: … (N tickets kept out of M read)) and the JSON carries the `perimetre` key; only with the option. A requested project with no ticket is counted in the Terminal; if no ticket of the requested projects is found, the calculation stops. `compare` adds a comparability note when two reports of the same series do not have the same scope (headcounts, however, may vary).
  - **`--history`** (optional, no path: a path given to the option is refused with exit code 2): adds the "Temps par segment" (time per segment) section from the state history collected by `history_jira.py` and read in the database (see "State history"); with no history in the database the command stops with exit code 1 ("aucun historique dans la base : lancez d'abord la collecte de l'historique", "no history in the database: run the history collection first"). The report gives, overall and per team, without ranking: the tickets that left each segment during the period (count, P50, P85, mean of cumulated time), the tickets still in a segment (count, age P50 and P85), the delivery lead time from the commitment point to resolution next to the one from creation, flow efficiency (the share of time in an active state, waiting states being those of `waiting_states`) and coverage, with a warning below 90%. Durations are differences of dates, in calendar days, initial state included, and the current state runs until the calculation date for an open ticket. A ticket's time is cumulated over all its visits to the segment. Without the option, the report is identical, and the added notions enter "Comment lire ce rapport" only with it.
  - **`segments`** and **`commitment_point`** in `flux.yaml` (optional): `segments` maps states to segments (state names are compared case-insensitively). Default values: `definition` (Definition, Definition - Ongoing, Definition - On Hold, Ready for Review (Definition), Analysis, Analysis - ongoing, Analysis - on hold, Ready for Enhancement Check), `ready` (Ready for Implementation), `implementation` (Implementation, Implementation - Ongoing, Implementation - On Hold, Analysis (Impl), Code Review (Impl), R&D Test (Impl)), `review_verification` (Ready for Review (Implementation), Review - Ongoing (Impl.), Review - On Hold (Impl.), Ready for Verification, Verification, Verification - Ongoing, Verification - On Hold) and `acceptance` (Ready for Acceptance, Documentation (Impl)). If you write `segments` in `flux.yaml`, it replaces the whole list. `commitment_point` (default `ready`) is the segment whose entry is the commitment point. `team_separator` (default `;`) separates the teams of a cell that contains several; team columns with the same name are added together. The per-team report assigns each ticket to the value of its first column, whole cell, and counts multi-team tickets separately. `uncounted_segments` (default `a_faire`: Open, Draft; `termine`: Closed, Resolved, Accepted & Released, Rejected) files the start and end states without computing any time for them, so that the list of states filed in no segment shows only the real omissions; like `segments`, writing it replaces the default list. States filed in no segment are listed in the report.
  - **Arrivals and departures**: the report counts, week by week, the tickets created (arrivals) next to throughput (departures), with the total of both and their difference, overall and per team, without ranking. Arrivals take the same types and the same exclusions as throughput; tickets created and then rejected count in arrivals, and never in departures. They depend on the export query.
  - **Batch closures**: with a key column and the time of day in the resolution date, the report counts, per project, the delivered tickets whose resolution falls in the same minute as that of at least 4 others of the same project (threshold of 5 tickets, adjustable), with their share of the project's throughput. Only the reasons listed in `batch_closure_resolutions` are tracked (`Done` by default; empty list: all reasons counted as delivered). This is a count only: these tickets stay in throughput, lead time and forecasts. Without a time of day in the date (date format without time), no group is possible and the report says so. `--batch-closure-resolutions "Done,Accepted & Released"` (or `tous` for all delivered reasons) replaces `batch_closure_resolutions` for one calculation, without touching `flux.yaml`. The section is titled "Fermetures groupées" in the report.
  - **Truncated first week**: when an export query in `flux.yaml` (`jql_export`, `jql_exports`) filters on `resolved >= -26w`, the window starts 26 weeks before the calculation date and the first week of the period is incomplete (items resolved before the window are missing). The "Arrivées et sorties" (Arrivals and departures) section says so: that week is to be excluded from comparisons. The calculation date serves as the date of the data.
  - **`waiting_states`** (optional) in `flux.yaml`: a list of states or patterns, for example `waiting_states: ["Ready for", "On Hold"]`. A state matches if it equals an entry, or starts or ends with it, case-insensitively ("Ready for" finds `Ready for Review`, "On Hold" finds `Implementation - On Hold`). Items in these states stay in the total WIP but are counted separately, as waiting queues: the report gives the active WIP, the waiting WIP and the waiting share, per team too, and a "Nature" column in the age per state. Without this setting, nothing changes. The setting describes a measure, it does not reclassify states: the `in_progress_states` and `todo_states` lists and the Jira status category keep deciding what is "in progress" and "to do".
  - **`--dormants`** (removed): the separate export of dormant tickets no longer exists; the option is refused with a message that points to `--dormant-from-history`.
  - **`--dormant-from-history`** (optional): infers the dormant tickets from the state history in the database: unfinished, created at least 365 days ago, no state change for at least 365 days, based on the date of the last transition (or of creation if there is none). With no history in the database the command stops with exit code 1, as for `--history`. A ticket with no history in the database is not counted as dormant (the Terminal and the report note give the number). The report then separates the active WIP, the waiting WIP (according to `waiting_states`) and the dormant WIP (a dormant ticket leaves the other two), with the share of each, overall and per team, and the dormant "to do" from the living "to do"; the age per state gains the "Dormants" and "Part dormante" (dormant share) columns. Little's law and the Monte Carlo forecast then apply to the non-dormant WIP (active and waiting); the result on the total WIP is given in a note, for comparison. A note in the report recalls the threshold (365 days) and the source (the state history).
  - **More than 10 teams** in the report: a note under the per-team table recalls that teams are not to be compared with one another (workflows, ticket sizes and types of work differ).
  - **Monthly follow-up**: each month, run the monthly run (it exports, imports, collects the history, then runs `compute` with the same `--name` for each series, for example `--name produit`), then `/monthly-flow produit` in Claude Code. Without a name, the command reads the `default_series` series of `flux.yaml` (for example `default_series: produit`) and asks for the name if the key is absent. "Keep this diagnostic" writes into `_bmad-output/pmzilla/flux/`, a folder ignored by Git. For a series of more than 10 teams, the Scaling posture reads the overview and Delivery comments on only 3 teams, with no ranking or comparison between teams. The first series has no evolution file: the command says so and reads only the flow report.
  The tests, on synthetic data (fixtures rendered by a test-only loader, no database): `uv run --with pyyaml --with numpy python -m unittest discover -s tests/flux_jira -v`.
- **Coaching journal**: the `Journal-Lab/` folder already exists; `Journal-Lab/journal.yaml` is created at the first session noted with `/journal add`. Harmless check: `uv run tools/journal_lab.py follow-ups` prints "aucune" (none) as long as the journal is empty.
- **Dashboard**: `uv run tools/dashboard.py --open` writes `~/PMZilla-Lab/tableau-de-bord.html` and opens it. It is then regenerated by `watch.sh` and by `evals_auto.sh`, without ever making them fail.

### `flux.yaml` settings

A single file, `Flux-Lab/flux.yaml` (outside Git), configures all the Jira scripts. `flux_jira.py` creates it on first run (the retention block's `max_file_age_days` keeps its name and now applies to the ageing-points files only); the other scripts use their default values if it is missing. Each setting below is read by the code; an absent setting takes the default value.

| Setting | Read by | Default | Role |
|---|---|---|---|
| `jira_site`, `jira_email`, `jira_token_type` | `history_jira.py`, `export_jira_api.py` | required for the API | address `https://<site>.atlassian.net`, email of the token's account, token type (`scoped`, the only value accepted) |
| `jira_cloud_id` | `history_jira.py`, `export_jira_api.py` | none | cloud identifier of the site, read by you in the browser (see "State history"); `history_jira.py test` compares it with the site's |
| `jira_token_expires`, `jira_token_warning_days` | `history_jira.py test` | none; `30` | expiry date of the token (`AAAA-MM-JJ`) and number of days before it from which `test` warns |
| `history_not_found_limit` | `history_jira.py collect` | whole number of at least 1; `3` | consecutive collections answered "not found" (HTTP 404) after which a ticket is marked gone and no longer queried (except with `--refresh`); migration `0012` |
| `declared_labels`, `cycles`, `patches`, `discovery_workflow`, `epic_types` | `export_jira_api.py`, `flux_jira.py cycles`, `flux_jira.py epics`, `epic_statuses.py` | none; `[Epic]` for `epic_types` | blocks described below, "Declared labels, cycles and Discovery Workflow" |
| `retention` | `flow_retention.py`, `export_jira_api.py`, `flow_import.py`, `history_jira.py` | see 14.8 | retention periods and label of the pre-purge backup |
| `jql_export` | `export_jira_api.py` | none | JQL query of the export (a single one) |
| `jql_exports` | `export_jira_api.py` | none | named queries (`nom: requête JQL`); `export` runs them all at each run into the raw layer of the database; takes precedence over `jql_export` |
| `export_team_field` | `export_jira_api.py` | none (no team) | identifier of the team field (`customfield_NNNNN`) |
| `export_team_header` | (unused) | `Team` | still accepted, no longer used: the export writes no file and so no header |
| `export_verification_field` | (unused) | none | still accepted, no longer used: the export no longer asks for the "Verification Required" field |
| `export_verification_header` | (unused) | `Verification Required` | still accepted, no longer used |
| `export_timezone` | `export_jira_api.py`, `flux_jira.py`, `dependencies_jira.py`, `history_jira.py` | `Europe/Paris` | time zone in which the dates are read from the database and counted as days |
| `export_points_field` | `export_jira_api.py` | none | identifier of the story points field (`customfield_NNNNN`), or a list tried in order (first non-empty value); without it, no "Story Points" column (see 14.1) |
| `flow_views_min_cell` | `flow_views.py` | `5` | masking threshold of the aggregated views of the `flow` schema; it can only raise it (see 14.1) |
| `date_format` | (removed) | | there is no date format any more (the dates come from the database); an old `date_format` or `format_date` key, and the French column names, are refused with the current name |
| `columns` | all | usual English and French names | possible names of each column (`key`, `type`, `status`, `category`, `created`, `resolved`, `resolution`, `category_changed`, `team`, `parent`); added names come before the default names |
| `in_progress_states`, `todo_states` | `flux_jira.py`, `dependencies_jira.py` | `In Progress`, `In Review`, `Testing`; `Open`, `To Do`, `Backlog` | classification of states; the status category, if the tickets carry it, decides first |
| `excluded_resolutions` | `flux_jira.py`, `dependencies_jira.py` | `Rejected`, `Won't Do`, `Duplicate`, `Cannot Reproduce`, `Obsolete` | closure reasons that are not deliveries |
| `portfolio_types` | `flux_jira.py`, `dependencies_jira.py`, `history_jira.py` | empty | portfolio-level types (`types_exclus` is the old name, refused) |
| `excluded_projects` | `flux_jira.py`, `dependencies_jira.py`, `history_jira.py` | empty | projects set aside (defects, support) |
| `dependency_excluded_projects` | `dependencies_jira.py` only | absent (the map uses `excluded_projects`) | projects left out of the dependency map; replaces `excluded_projects` for the map when present, an empty list included; `--exclude` replaces both |
| `scope_projects` | `dependencies_jira.py` only | empty (all) | projects studied; `flux_jira.py` does not read it (`--scope` option) |
| `waiting_states` | `flux_jira.py` | empty | states (or prefixes and suffixes) counted as waiting queues |
| `segments`, `uncounted_segments`, `commitment_point` | `flux_jira.py --history`, `history_jira.py check` | five segments (definition, ready, implementation, review and verification, acceptance); `todo` and `done` outside the calculation; `ready` | segments of the time per state (details above) |
| `batch_closure_threshold`, `batch_closure_resolutions` | `flux_jira.py` | `5`; `Done` | threshold (integer of at least 2) and resolution reasons of the "Fermetures groupées" section |
| `boards` | `flow_board.py` | none | series shown by the visual dashboard, with their dependency scopes and baselines (see "Visual flow dashboard") |
| `team_separator` | `flux_jira.py`, `history_jira.py` | `;` | separator of teams in a cell that contains several |
| `blocking_link_types`, `waiting_link_types` | `dependencies_jira.py` | `Blocks`, `Bloque`, `Blocage`, `Prerequisite`, `Prérequis`; `Depends`, `Dependance`, `Dépendance`, `Dependency` | link types counted as a dependency |
| `project_names` | `dependencies_jira.py` | empty | readable name per project key (`--by project`) |
| `default_series` | `/monthly-flow` command (read by Claude Code, not by a script) | none | series used when `/monthly-flow` is run without a name |

**Settings renamed.** The keys of `flux.yaml` have English names (the table above). The old French names are refused, with a message that gives the current name and the line. A key written twice in the same block, or one setting written under its old and its new name (for example `types_exclus` next to `portfolio_types`), is refused with the path of the key and the numbers of the two lines, never a value: keep one of the two lines. To rewrite your file once: `uv run tools/flux_jira.py config migrate --dry-run` lists the keys that would change (names only, never a value), then `uv run tools/flux_jira.py config migrate` rewrites `Flux-Lab/flux.yaml` keeping its comments and keeps the old file as `flux.yaml.avant-migration`. Each change is listed with its line number and the full path of the key as written in your file (for example `L13 : colonnes.debut -> category_changed`). If two keys of the same block would end up with the same name (for example `types_exclus`, the pre-rename name of `types_portefeuille`, written next to it, or a key written twice), the command writes nothing and lists the lines in conflict: keep one of them, then run it again. Run it yourself in the Terminal, not through Claude Code. The command-line options were renamed the same way (`--nom` is `--name`, `--perimetre` is `--scope`, and so on); the old options are refused, with a message that gives the current one.

**Organisation referential (`Flux-Lab/organisation.yaml`).** An optional file, outside Git and unreadable by Claude Code, that describes sites, departments, Mission Teams and teams; it replaces the team table `equipes-assets.yaml`, which the tools no longer read (see "Team translation" below). The export translates the Jira team identifiers through it, together with the team catalogue, and the flow and dependency reports can group by Mission Team or site. This document gives its keys only; every value stays in your file. Any key outside this list is refused (no field about a person), with the path and the line number, never a value.

```yaml
source_date: <AAAA-MM-JJ>
sites: [<site>, ...]
departments:
  - name: <département>
    site: <site>                        # one of the declared sites
    headcount: <effectif>
    mission_teams:                      # or teams: [<équipe>, ...] (exactly one of the two)
      - name: <mission team>
        discovery_workflow_pilot: true  # optional, boolean
        teams: [<équipe>, ...]
teams:
  - name: <équipe>
    headcount: <effectif>
    site: <site>                        # optional
    jira_team_ids: [<identifiant>, ...] # optional
    type: <sous-type de Team>           # optional, forces the type deduced from the catalogue (see below)
cross_cutting_teams: [<équipe>, ...]    # optional
```

**Team translation (`Flux-Lab/equipes-jira.yaml`, team-mapping change).** When the team field returns Assets object identifiers, the export translates them through two sources read together: the referential above (the organisation's teams, with their headcount) and the **team catalogue** `Flux-Lab/equipes-jira.yaml`, a complete copy of the Assets team list (every site, every status). Both files stay outside Git and are denied to Claude Code. The former table `equipes-assets.yaml` is never read any more: while the file exists, `export`, `diagnose` and `check-teams` print `ancienne table des équipes plus lue : supprimer equipes-assets.yaml` ("former team table no longer read: delete equipes-assets.yaml").

```yaml
teams:
  - name: <libellé>                       # the Assets label
    jira_team_ids: [<identifiant>, ...]
    type: <sous-type de Team>             # optional: Development Team, Verification Team, Product Management Team or Other Team
    obsolete: true                        # optional; written by migrate-teams for an inactive or discarded team
```

- **Assets is the source of truth.** `migrate-teams` imports every team of the list, the organisation's teams included, with its identifier, type and status; nothing is sorted entry by entry. Objects `inactive` or `discarded` are written with `obsolete: true` (so that old tickets stay recognised and never make `--strict-teams` fail); `draft` is treated as active; an entry that mixes an active and an inactive object is written without the flag and listed.
- **An identifier may be in both files.** The referential gives the name (translation), the catalogue gives the type and the status. The one refusal left: a catalogue entry named like a referential team that holds an identifier no referential team lists (exit code 1, key paths and lines, never a value): add the identifier to that referential team, or correct the referential name.
- **Type of a team.** The `type` key is optional and is never to be written by default: the tools deduce the type of a referential team from the type that the catalogue gives to its identifiers (none when they differ or when none is in the catalogue); a `type` key in the referential only forces a value, above the catalogue type. No tool writes the referential, and no type is ever guessed from a name.
- **Counted apart at export.** `teams outside the organisation: <n> identifiers, <t> tickets` (catalogue identifiers that no referential team lists), `tickets on obsolete teams: <t>`, and, for identifiers in neither source, `team identifiers in neither the referential nor the catalogue: <n>, on <t> tickets` followed by `identifiers to add: <ids>`. `--strict-teams` exits with 3 only for identifiers in neither source. Without any identifier in the two sources the export stops (exit code 1, nothing written) and prints the structure of both files.
- **Confidentiality.** A catalogue label is a team name, under the rules of team names: outside Git, never in a tracked file, an issue or a console line of a control. The controls print counts, identifiers and key paths only. The only exception is the proposal of `migrate-teams` for the sensitive-names list, printed in your Terminal.

Two commands, **for you alone, in the Terminal** (they refuse to start inside a Claude Code session with exit code 3, make no Jira call, and are denied to Claude Code):

- `uv run tools/export_jira_api.py migrate-teams --from-assets Flux-Lab/equipes-assets-complet-<AAAA-MM-JJ>.csv` writes the catalogue once (permissions 600; it refuses to overwrite an existing one) from the complete Assets list. The accepted file is `equipes-assets-complet-<AAAA-MM-JJ>.csv` of the flow folder, UTF-8, comma-separated, with a header holding exactly the five columns `id,type,libelle,nom_court,statut` in any order (any other column is refused, so that an attribute about a person can never be read). `id` is the numeric identifier of the Assets object (written as read, never converted), `type` one of the four exact names (`Development Team`, `Verification Team`, `Product Management Team`, `Other Team`; outer spaces are cut), `statut` one of `active`, `draft`, `inactive`, `discarded`. The whole file is checked before anything is written; a refusal names a column, a position or a line number, never a value. An empty `nom_court` is accepted (the entry is named by its `libelle`, which is the only name the catalogue ever holds) and the command warns with the count of those lines per status, draft apart; an empty `id`, `type`, `statut` or `libelle` is refused with the count of lines concerned and the first line. The command prints counts and identifiers (objects read per status, entries written, types written, entries written obsolete, mixed statuses), lists the identifiers of entries named like a referential team (to place in the referential before the next export), and, when the former table exists, compares it with the list in counts only. It then **proposes** the distinctive labels to add to the sensitive-names list (at least 6 characters, with a word that is neither of three letters or fewer nor a common word); it never writes them to a file. The link between the field and Assets (object schema 2, Team type id 9, sub-types 10 to 13) is documentation only: no tool needs it, and extracting the list through the Assets API would be a change of its own.
- `uv run tools/export_jira_api.py check-teams` reads the referential, the catalogue, the former table and the latest Assets list (chosen by the date in its file name, never by modification date; only its file name is printed) and prints counts, identifiers and key paths. Exit code 4 when identifiers of the former table are covered by neither source, 1 on a refusal, 0 otherwise; the signals below never change the code. Your review covers these gaps only: referential identifiers absent from Assets; referential teams with an identifier inactive or discarded in Assets; referential teams without any `jira_team_ids`; teams whose identifiers have different types in the catalogue; the forced types (each listed with the Assets type, those that differ from the deduced type, those no longer needed, to remove); the types changed in Assets since the catalogue was written (read before any rebuild of the catalogue); and the catalogue entries seen on the stored tickets (a team of the organisation filed outside the referential by mistake): `check-teams` reads no export file for it, it reads the view `flow.v_team_tickets_seen` of the database (migration `0013`: one count of tickets per team name, tickets out of the export left out, not masked), under the read-only role of the flow, connected like the report readers. That view is readable by that role only, never by the views role, the session or the dashboards; the entries are matched by label, and the "to review" line gives the sum of the counts per entry, worded "tickets counted per entry" (a ticket carrying two entries to review counts twice). With the database unreachable, or with no stored ticket, the review is skipped with one line and the exit code is that of the other checks. You alone run `check-teams`, after applying migration `0013` (`uv run tools/db.py status` shows no pending migration). Without an Assets list, one line says which three list controls were skipped.

**Team names extract (`Flux-Lab/equipes-lisibles.yaml`).** The organisation allows team names to be stored as they are and read by Claude Code, through two routes only: the extract described here and the two views of 14.1. The extract is the readable copy of the two closed files above. A third command, **for you alone, in the Terminal** (it refuses to start inside a Claude Code session with exit code 3, before it reads any file; it makes no Jira call and opens no database session):

- `uv run tools/export_jira_api.py extract-teams [--language fr|en]` reads the referential and the catalogue with their strict readers and writes `Flux-Lab/equipes-lisibles.yaml` (permissions 600, written atomically, replaced at each run, ignored by Git). It holds, for each referential team, its name, its Jira team identifiers, its effective type and a "without identifier" flag; for each catalogue entry, its name, its identifiers, its type, the obsolete flag and an "outside the referential" flag (no referential team lists any of its identifiers); and the date of the run. It leaves out everything else: no Mission Team, site, department, headcount, pilot flag or comment, and no text of the source files. A refused source keeps its message, exits 1 and writes nothing; with one source missing, the other is extracted; with none, it exits 1 and writes nothing. The console prints counts only (teams of the referential and how many have no identifier, catalogue entries), never a name.

The extract is as fresh as its last run: run it again after editing the referential or the catalogue. Claude Code may read it, and only it; the referential, the catalogue, the sensitive-names list and the tables stay closed. A team name read this way never enters a file tracked by Git, a commit message, an issue or anything on GitHub (generic designations only): the `pre-commit` hook and `tools/gh_issue.py` refuse the distinctive names of the extract (section 9, "Safety net on names"). The reading of the Jira team identifiers stored under a catalogue label or under the unknown-team label goes through the view `v_team_jira_ids` (14.1).

After the merge, in a separate session and before the next export: run `migrate-teams`, add the proposed labels to `Flux-Lab/noms-sensibles.txt`, run `check-teams` and review the gaps, clean the former table (for each identifier it lists as not covered: place it in the referential, add it by hand to the catalogue with `obsolete: true`, or remove it from the table), check that `check-teams` then `export --strict-teams` exit with 0 the same day, and delete `Flux-Lab/equipes-assets.yaml`.

Grouping options: `uv run tools/flux_jira.py compute --name <series> --by team|mission-team|site` and `uv run tools/dependencies_jira.py compute --name <series> --by team|project|mission-team|site` (both read the database; export files are refused). The teams of the export that the referential does not describe form a group "hors référentiel" in French, "outside the referential" in English. `export` prints `équipes du référentiel sans ticket : <n>` (a count) when a team of the referential has no ticket.

**Declared labels, cycles and Discovery Workflow (blocks of `flux.yaml`, structure only).** The labels and versions are read in the database path only (see 14.1 and `application.md`, section 9). The export writes only the labels you declare, never the plain `Labels` column; an undeclared label with a declared prefix is only counted. Everything between `<>` below is a placeholder; the real values stay in your `flux.yaml`.

```yaml
declared_labels:                 # role -> prefix and labels; each label starts with the prefix of its role
  up_merge:                      # exactly two labels (the legal one, then the hotfix one)
    prefix: <préfixe>
    labels: [<étiquette-légal>, <étiquette-hotfix>]
  cycle:
    prefix: <préfixe>
    labels: [<étiquette>]
    cycle_keys: {<étiquette>: <AAAA-C1>}   # optional (epic flow report): the key of `cycles` that the label plans, never read from its text
  epic_review:
    prefix: <préfixe>
    labels: [<étiquette>]
  pilot_domain:                  # epic flow report: exactly one label, the pilot domain
    prefix: <préfixe>
    labels: [<étiquette>]
  ready:                         # epic flow report: at most one label; empty or absent = Ready label not configured (the Ready column is used)
    prefix: <préfixe>
    labels: []
  confirmation:                  # epic flow report: each label says whether the epic is taken into its cycle
    prefix: <préfixe>
    labels: [<étiquette>, <étiquette>]
    meanings: {<étiquette>: taken_in, <étiquette>: not_taken_in}
cycles:                          # key YYYY-Cn (n from 1 to 6) or YYYY-EOY; one cycle = one version = one fixVersion value
  <AAAA-C1>:
    fix_version: <version>
    start: <AAAA-MM-JJ>          # a Monday
    end: <AAAA-MM-JJ>            # a Friday
    up_merge_provision: <part>   # optional
    base_version: true           # optional: the cycle is the annual base version
patches:                         # key YYYY-Qn (n from 1 to 4) -> patch versions, none of them in `cycles`
  <AAAA-Q1>: [<version>]
discovery_workflow:
  columns:                       # column -> statuses (a status in one column only)
    <colonne>: [<statut>, ...]
  start_column: <colonne>        # optional
  review_passed_columns: [<colonne>, ...]   # optional
  ready_column: <colonne>        # optional (epic flow report): its first entry ends "idea to Ready" when no Ready label is configured
  active_columns: [<colonne>, ...]   # optional (epic flow report): columns where an epic counts as Discovery work in progress; not the Ready column
  validated_on: <AAAA-MM-JJ>     # without it, the mapping is not validated: submissions only
epic_types: [<type>]             # types read by epic_statuses.py; [Epic] by default
```

Every block is checked at load, with the path and line of the fault, never a value. Set `base_version` yourself if the release manager confirms that the first cycle of the year is the annual base version. Reports: `uv run tools/flux_jira.py cycles --name <série> [--scope <clés>]` reads the database only (a CSV given as argument is refused) and writes `Flux-Lab/rapports/cycles-<série>-AAAA-MM-JJ.md` and `.json` (cycle keys only, never a version or a label name); `uv run tools/epic_statuses.py [--history]` reads the tickets and the history from the database only (an export file or a path given to `--history` is refused, exit code 2) and writes `Flux-Lab/rapports/statuts-epics-AAAA-MM-JJ.md` (counts per status, `masqué` (`masked` in English) under five, no key; without `validated_on`, the report says the mapping is not validated). Run both yourself in the Terminal, not through Claude Code: `epic_statuses.py` refuses to start in a Claude Code session (exit code 3).

**Epic flow report.** `uv run tools/flux_jira.py epics --name <série> [--language fr|en]` reads the database only (a CSV given as argument is refused, exit code 1) and writes `Flux-Lab/rapports/epics-<série>-AAAA-MM-JJ.md` and `.json`: for the epics that carry the pilot-domain label on the run day, one row for the domain, one per team (name order, never ordered by a figure) and a `(no team)` row, for five measures (idea to Ready, passages in Epic Review, planned cycle and carry-over, epics per board column, age of epics in progress). Counts under five and percentiles over fewer than five epics read `masqué` (`masked` in English); when exactly one team cell is masked, the smallest other one is masked too and reads the same; the JSON cells are `{value, state}` objects. The history collection (`history_jira.py collect`) queries the epic types even when they are portfolio types and prints `epics queried: <n>`; after declaring a label late, run it once with `--refresh` for epics finished earlier. Without a validated mapping (or without `active_columns`, or without a Ready column while no Ready label is configured) the report is reduced to the passages, the carry-over and the count per Jira status. Run it yourself in the Terminal: it refuses to start in a Claude Code session (exit code 3). No team, label or version name appears on the console or in a tracked file; team names appear only in the report files of `Flux-Lab/rapports/`.

### Flow metrics: procedure, options and reading

**What it is for.** To compute locally, from the tickets in the database, the throughput per week, the lead time, the WIP, the age of in-progress items (per state and per team), arrivals and departures, Monte Carlo forecasts and, with the state history, the time per segment; then to have them interpreted by the Delivery posture (action **DF**). Only aggregates are produced: no ticket, no title, no person. The script reads only the type, state, dates, closure reason, team and key of each ticket (the key is used to link, in memory), and writes no key, title or description. It reads the database under the read-only role; there is no file to give.

**Procedure.**

1. **Bring the database up to date.** The monthly run does it (`export_jira_api.py export` into the raw layer, `flow_import.py`, `history_jira.py collect`; see "Jira API export" and "Monthly procedure through the API"). The export reads every ticket of every named query at each run and writes no file.
2. **Run the tool in the Terminal.** `statuses` and `compute` are for you alone: a Claude Code session is refused (exit code 3). An export file, `--source` or a path given to `--history` is refused (exit code 2).
3. **Check the data.** `uv run tools/flux_jira.py statuses` prints the recognized columns, the period covered, then the states and types with their ticket counts.
4. **Classify the states in `Flux-Lab/flux.yaml`**: `in_progress_states` (from the commitment point on; a "ready to plan" state stays in `todo_states`), `todo_states` (everything before commitment) and `excluded_resolutions`. Resolved tickets are recognized by their resolution date. This classification is a reading convention, not the Jira category: a state that is "In Progress" in Jira can be filed under "to do" and then leaves the WIP. `statuses` flags unclassified states.
5. **Calculate.** `uv run tools/flux_jira.py compute --name equipe-a` writes `Flux-Lab/rapports/flux-<nom>-AAAA-MM-JJ.md` (and `.json`; `AAAA-MM-JJ` is the date, YYYY-MM-DD). Also run a 10-week calculation (`--weeks 10 --name equipe-a-recent`): it gives a more cautious forecast than the default window.
6. **Have the report interpreted.** In Claude Code, `/pmzilla-delivery-bmad` then "DF à partir de `Flux-Lab/rapports/<rapport>.md`" (DF from the report), giving the scope, the decision at stake and the meaning of the states. The posture keeps its notes in memory, asks its context questions and passes the diagnostic to Critic. "Keep this diagnostic" saves it in `_bmad-output/pmzilla/`.
7. **Start again after the next monthly run** to follow the evolution: at the end of `compute`, the script compares the new report with the previous one of the same `--name` and writes `Flux-Lab/rapports/evolution-<nom>-AAAA-MM-JJ.md` (and `.json`). Then `/monthly-flow <nom>` in Claude Code.

**Options of `compute`** (`uv run tools/flux_jira.py compute --help`). `--history`, `--dormant-from-history` and `--scope` are described above, in the bullets of "Flow metrics".

| Option | Effect |
|---|---|
| `--name NOM` | short name of the team or scope, used in the report name and for the comparison |
| `--weeks N` | complete weeks analyzed (26 by default) |
| `--target N` | number of items to finish for the "in how many weeks" forecast (by default, the WIP) |
| `--target-date AAAA-MM-JJ` | date for the "how many items by then" forecast |
| `--types Story,Bug` | ticket types kept, separated by commas |
| `--teams-only` | excludes tickets with no team (a separate flow, absent from the WIP) |
| `--language fr\|en` | language of the report and of the messages (French by default; the old `--langue` is refused). The other scripts of the flow (`compare`, `statuses`, `dependencies_jira.py`, `history_jira.py`, `export_jira_api.py`, `segment_sample.py`, `journal_lab.py`, `critic_mirror.py`, `dashboard.py`) have the same option |
| `--no-comparison` | does not compare with the previous report of the same name |
| `--points` | also writes `Flux-Lab/tableaux/points-<nom>-AAAA-MM-JJ.json`: one point per in-progress ticket (team, state, segment, age, nature), without key, for the dashboard's Aging WIP; outside Git, outside the reports (which stay aggregates) |

**Follow-up over time.** `uv run tools/flux_jira.py compare --name equipe-a` redoes the comparison on demand, without a new calculation; `--before AAAA-MM-JJ` and `--after AAAA-MM-JJ` choose the two reports (by default, the two most recent). The evolution file contains "Comparabilité" (Comparability: what prevents drawing conclusions: different period, types or scope, a report in an old format with a skipped section), "Ce qui a bougé" (What moved: throughput, lead time P85, WIP, Little's law, aging, batch closures, teams that change, each beyond a threshold set in the script), then the throughput detail. A gap under the threshold does not prove stability, and each signal is a question to put to the teams, not a conclusion. The reading by Claude Code (`/monthly-flow`, team series or product series) is described in `.claude/commands/monthly-flow.md`.

**The `Flux-Lab` folder** (in the workspace, content excluded from Git except an empty `.gitkeep`):

| Path | Content | Readable by Claude Code |
|---|---|---|
| `Flux-Lab/exports/` | no longer written (database-only): CSV files left from the CSV route, counted and deleted by `flow_retention.py` | no |
| `Flux-Lab/historique/` | no longer written: the former state history cache file, counted and deleted by `flow_retention.py` | no |
| `Flux-Lab/equipes-jira.yaml` | team catalogue (complete copy of the Assets team list, written by `migrate-teams`) | no |
| `Flux-Lab/equipes-assets-complet-AAAA-MM-JJ.csv` | complete Assets team list, read by `migrate-teams` and `check-teams` | no |
| `Flux-Lab/equipes-assets.yaml` | former table of object identifier to team label, no longer read (to delete) | no |
| `Flux-Lab/equipes-lisibles.yaml` | team-names extract written by `export_jira_api.py extract-teams` (team names, Jira team identifiers, type, flags, date; permissions 600, outside Git) | yes |
| `Flux-Lab/tableaux/` | Aging WIP points (`points-…json`), HTML dashboards, `anonymes.yaml` (mapping of anonymous names) | no |
| `Flux-Lab/noms-sensibles.txt` | names that the `pre-commit` hook refuses in a commit | no |
| `Flux-Lab/rapports/` | aggregate reports (`flux-…`, `evolution-…`, `dependances-…`, in `.md` and `.json`), with no ticket | yes |
| `Flux-Lab/flux.yaml` | settings | yes |

**What the flow report contains**: at the top, the scope (only with `--scope`); throughput per week and closure reasons; arrivals and departures, overall and per team, then, when some tickets carry several teams (and the aggregation is by team), a "Tickets partagés par équipe" (Shared tickets by team) section: a ticket counts once, for the first value of its first team column, and each team row gives the tickets attributed to it while shared and the tickets it carries that are attributed elsewhere, a cell under 5 written `masqué` (`masked` in English), rows in name order, with no key and no pair of teams (the glossary then adds a "Tickets partagés" entry to "Comment lire ce rapport"); lead time, overall and per type; WIP per state (active, waiting, dormant when configured); the age of in-progress items per state and per threshold (30, 90, 180, 365 and 730 days), the queues before commitment; Little's law; the breakdown per team, with each team's weekly series and the note on multi-team tickets; with `--history`, the "Temps par segment" (segment exits, age in the segment, delivery lead time, flow efficiency, coverage); the Monte Carlo forecasts (10,000 simulations); a "Comment lire ce rapport" section that defines the terms; the limits.

**Limits** (repeated in each report):

- lead time starts at ticket creation, backlog included: it is not a cycle time;
- age starts at entry into the Jira "In Progress" category, which can include analysis and definition: it is not a development time;
- without `--history`, no time per state: the report does not say how long an item has waited in its current state;
- throughput counts closed tickets, not delivered value;
- forecasts assume that future throughput resembles that of the period and that items are of comparable size, and they depend on the window chosen;
- over a few weeks, a per-team throughput is a very small sample: do not present per-team figures as a ranking.

### Segment sample (`segment_sample.py`)

To look, in the state history, at what hides behind a time per segment (for example a P50 for "Revue et vérification" (Review and verification) in the "Temps par segment" section of the flow report). The script reads the tickets and the collected history in the database, `flux.yaml` and the calculation functions of `flux_jira.py`: same tickets, same segment states, same period. For you alone, in the Terminal (a Claude Code session is refused, exit code 3).

1. **Run**: `uv run tools/segment_sample.py --today AAAA-MM-JJ` (the report date of the calculation to reproduce: the period depends on it). An export file or a path given to `--history` is refused (exit code 2); with no history in the database the tool stops with exit code 1. It writes nothing to disk.
2. **Check the header**: the number of tickets that left the segment and its P50 must match the flow report. If not, the scope differs (data brought up to date since, date, `--scope`, `--types`): do not read the sample.
3. **Read**: the states that make up the segment; the longest ones (time cumulated over all visits); a random draw, with a fixed seed (`--seed`, 42 by default), among the tickets between P40 and P60; separately, the oldest ones still in the segment. Per ticket: key, project, days, visits, entry and exit of the longest visit, origin and destination state.
4. **Options**: `--by-state` adds the time per state (total, share of the segment, P50, P85), the sample detail and the batch exits of the longest ones; `--starts` adds the transitions from "Ready for Verification" to "Verification - Ongoing" per project (transitions, distinct days, share on the busiest 20% of days, concentration at equal active days against 1000 random draws over the working days, French public holidays removed (expected share, 95th percentile, "yes"/"no"), distinct active days observed against the draws (expected, 5th percentile, ratio, "yes"/"no" for fewer active days than chance), P50 of the wait), without any key, for projects with at least `--min-passes` transitions (20 by default); `--exclude-batch-closures` removes from the population the tickets that left the segment in a batch closure, with the rule of the "Fermetures groupées" section of `flux_jira.py` (same function: a delivered ticket resolved in the same minute as at least `batch_closure_threshold` - 1 others of the same project, 5 by default, among the resolutions of `batch_closure_resolutions`, "Done" by default; it needs the time in the resolution date): the output states at the top how many tickets and how many "Ready for Verification" to "Verification - Ongoing" transitions were discarded, and every section (population, time per state, transitions per project, active days against chance) is computed without them; without the option the output does not change; `--segment`, `--longest`, `--near-median`, `--oldest`, `--weeks`, `--scope`, `--types`, `--history` complete the set.

The output contains only keys, projects and states: no title and no person. Keys do not enter any file tracked by Git. The tests (`tests/segment_sample/`) run on fixtures rendered by a test-only loader.

### Dependency map

`tools/dependencies_jira.py` draws the dependencies between teams from the tickets and links in the database, on the same principle as `flux_jira.py`: ticket keys are used only to link tickets together, in memory, and are never written; the report (`Flux-Lab/rapports/dependances-<nom>-AAAA-MM-JJ.md`, and `.json`) contains only aggregates, readable by Claude Code. The script is run from `tools/`, without a copy in `~/PMZilla-Lab/`, by you alone in the Terminal (a Claude Code session is refused, exit code 3; an export file or `--source` is refused, exit code 2). Project and team names appear only in `Flux-Lab` (outside Git); the examples below are generic.

1. **Export from Jira through the API into the database.** Define the named queries in `jql_exports` of `flux.yaml` and run `uv run tools/export_jira_api.py export` (the monthly run does it): every ticket of every query is read in full at each run and written into the raw layer, then imported (`flow_import.py`). There is no file, no batch to merge and no 10,000-ticket truncation hint any more. For example:
   - **Query A**, the portfolio project (the one with the Epics) limited to open tickets, plus two cross-cutting projects: `(project = PORT AND resolution IS EMPTY) OR project in (TRANS1, TRANS2)`, where `PORT`, `TRANS1` and `TRANS2` are generic keys to replace with yours;
   - **Query B**, all the other projects of the product: `project in (ABC, DEF, GHI) AND (resolved >= -26w OR resolution IS EMPTY)`.
   Also take the projects that yours are linked to, otherwise the team on the other side of a link stays unknown. The export reads the key, the status category, the type, the status, the dates, the resolution, the parent (to know whether an old Epic still has active children), the team and the typed links (blocks and is blocked by).
2. **Set the scope in `Flux-Lab/flux.yaml`**, the file of `flux_jira.py` (created at its first run; without it, the script takes its default values):
   - `scope_projects`: the projects studied, for example `[ABC, DEF]` (empty: all those in the database). The other projects serve only to locate the team on the other side of a link: their internal dependencies and their lead time are not counted;
   - `excluded_projects`: the projects whose links are not dependencies between teams (defects, support, tests), counted separately, outside the matrix;
   - `dependency_excluded_projects` (optional, R-72): the projects left out of the map only, a list of keys written in this local file and never in a tracked file. Precedence: the `--exclude` option of the run, else this setting when it is present (an empty list excludes nothing), else `excluded_projects`; the two lists are never merged. Without the key the map behaves as before. The flow report never reads it. A project that is both in `scope_projects` and excluded stays excluded. A value that is not a list of non-empty keys stops every tool that reads `flux.yaml` (exit code 1, key and line named, never the value);
   - `portfolio_types`: the portfolio-level ticket types, for example `[Epic, Initiative]`. A link between one of these tickets and a ticket of another level is backlog decomposition: it leaves the map, counted separately. A link between two tickets of this level stays a dependency;
   - `blocking_link_types` and `waiting_link_types`: the link types counted as a dependency. For `blocking_link_types`, the outward direction reads "blocks" (by default `Blocks`, `Bloque`, `Prerequisite` and their variants); for `waiting_link_types`, it reads "depends on" (by default `Depends`, `Dependency` and their variants). The link label on a Jira ticket gives the direction: check it before trusting "who waits for whom";
   - `project_names`: a readable name per project key, for example `ABC: Domain A`, used when the report is aggregated by project.
3. **Diagnose the data.** `uv run tools/dependencies_jira.py diagnose` prints the recognized columns, the link types and their count (ignored or counted), the share of links that leave the data read (above 30%, widen the query), the excluded projects, the decomposition links and the tickets with no team. Option: `--scope ABC,DEF` (or `tous`) replaces `scope_projects` of `flux.yaml` for this command. Option: `--exclude ABC,DEF` (or `none` / `aucun` to exclude nothing) replaces both exclusion lists for this command and writes nothing; an option with no key stops with exit code 2 before any read. The diagnosis, and the report, give a source line ("Projects excluded from the map: n (source: ...)"), with the number of projects and never their keys: read it before comparing two maps, since the list decides which delivered tickets count as having an external dependency.
4. **Calculate.** `uv run tools/dependencies_jira.py compute --name perimetre` writes the report: team-by-team matrix (open dependencies, including between Epics, of unknown state, all), Mermaid graph, loops, current blockings (with the share of Epics and the "Même date d'entrée" (same entry date) column) and those older than a year, waits toward an item outside the data read, lead time for the same type. A dependency toward a ticket absent from the database is "à état inconnu" (of unknown state): it is never counted as open. A dependency between two Epics (types listed in `portfolio_types`, for example `[Epic, Initiative]`) measures portfolio sequencing, not a wait for work: its age is that of the Epic's life, and at product scale the map first describes this sequencing. The "Même date d'entrée" column flags, for a row of at least 3 items, those that entered in-progress on the same day, and the "Âges à vérifier" (ages to check) warning lists them: this is the probable sign of a batch status change (planning, migration), which overestimates the wait. Items with neither an entry date nor a creation date are ignored in this count. Options: `--by projet` aggregates by Jira project instead of team (a readable view at product scale; the file then carries `-projets` in its name), `--scope` as for `diagnose`, `--weeks 26` for the lead time period, `--keys` to display the keys of the blockings in the Terminal only, writing nothing.
5. **Check the old Epics.** The "Epics anciens : vivants ou abandonnés ?" (Old Epics: alive or abandoned?) section of the report classifies the Epics open for more than a year and caught in an open link: alive (a delivery child changed state or was delivered in the last 90 days), with inactive children, or with no delivery child. An RN Task (a portfolio-level child, for example) or a closure by rejection or cancellation does not count as a delivery, because it is often a batch cleanup. The classification sees only the tickets in the database: for the Epics that are not alive, run `compute` again with `--keys`: the script prints in the Terminal (nothing is written) a Jira query `parent in (EPIC-1, EPIC-2, ...) ORDER BY updated DESC`. Paste it in Jira's advanced search: it lists all the children of these Epics, finished ones included, with their last update. You then know which are empty, entirely rejected, dormant or active. The count to share keeps neither titles nor keys.
6. **Have the report read.** In Claude Code, `/dependencies perimetre`: the Scaling posture opens with a verdict on what the map allows concluding, then reads the loaded pairs, the loops and the interaction modes (see the README, section "Dependency map (`dependencies_jira.py`)").

**Check**: `diagnose` prints "Mode : orienté" (Mode: directed) if the links are typed, "non orienté" (undirected) otherwise, and a "Tickets sans équipe" (tickets with no team) line. The report exists in `Flux-Lab/rapports/`.

### State history (collection)

`tools/history_jira.py` collects the history of the status field alone for each ticket (transition date, origin state, destination state), the first step of measuring time per state. The organization validated this measure over the whole product scope, provided it is aggregated by team and carries no individual data: the script discards the author and every other field as soon as it reads the response. The time-per-state calculation is then done with `flux_jira.py compute --history` ("Temps par segment" section of the report), and dormant tickets are inferred from the same history with `--dormant-from-history` (see "Flow metrics"). Since the database-only change the history lives in the database: `collect` reads the tickets there, and writes the transitions, the has-history flag and the label and version periods in one transaction, under the import role; no cache file is read or written. Run the import first: the collection reads the tickets it derived.

1. **Create a token** in Atlassian (account management, API tokens). Only a "limited scope" token (scopes) is accepted: the classic mode is refused (exit code 1, `jeton classique refusé : utiliser un jeton à scopes`). The recommended scope is the classic scope `read:jira-work` alone: it covers the search, the history and the field list, and no user scope is needed (the tools ask Jira for no person). Granular scopes are an option, to be checked before the switch (reports describe failures of the search with them): check the list in Atlassian's documentation, then run the resource probe of step 4. An HTTP 403 on the first try signals a missing permission. Note the expiry date shown by Atlassian when you create the token (step 3).
2. **Store it in the macOS Keychain**, under the service `pmzilla-jira`, **in this order** (the clipboard trap: if you copy the token before pasting the command, the clipboard holds the command instead of the token):
   1. **First paste the command in the Terminal, without pressing Enter**:
      ```bash
      security add-generic-password -U -a "$USER" -s pmzilla-jira -w "$(pbpaste | tr -d '\r\n ')"
      ```
      (`pbpaste` reads the clipboard; `tr` removes the line breaks and spaces that copying may add.)
   2. **Only then, copy the token** with Atlassian's "Copy" button: the clipboard now holds the token.
   3. **Then press Enter.**

   Never use `security`'s interactive prompt (`-w` without a value): it truncates the token to 128 characters. Do not pass the token as a plain argument either (it would stay in the shell history). **Check without displaying it**: the following command shows only the length and shape of the token, never the token (length of about 192 characters, starts with `ATATT`, ends with `=` followed by 8 characters):
   ```bash
   t=$(security find-generic-password -s pmzilla-jira -w); echo "longueur : ${#t}"; [[ $t == ATATT* ]] && echo "début ATATT : oui"; [[ $t =~ '=.{8}$' ]] && echo "fin = + 8 caractères : oui"; unset t
   ```
   (The command prints "longueur : …" (length), "début ATATT : oui" (starts with ATATT: yes) and "fin = + 8 caractères : oui" (ends with = + 8 characters: yes).) If one of the three checks is missing, start again (`-U` replaces the existing entry). The token is read only from the Keychain: it is never displayed, logged, written to a file or repeated in an error message. Once the command has run, copy some harmless text so as not to leave the token in the clipboard.
3. **Set `Flux-Lab/flux.yaml`**:
   - `jira_site`: `https://<site>.atlassian.net`;
   - `jira_email`: the email of the token's account;
   - `jira_token_type`: `scoped` (the default and the only accepted value: calls go through `https://api.atlassian.com/ex/jira/<cloudId>`);
   - `jira_cloud_id`: the cloud identifier of the site, which you read yourself in the browser (Atlassian administration, or the tenant information page of your site) and write in `flux.yaml`; this document never carries a value. Calls use this setting and no longer read the tenant information at every run; `test` reads it once to compare, and a difference stops with exit code 1 (`cloudId différent de celui du site`);
   - `jira_token_expires` (date `AAAA-MM-JJ`) and `jira_token_warning_days` (integer of at least 1, 30 by default): the expiry date of the token and the number of days before it from which `test` warns.
4. **Check the connection**: `uv run tools/history_jira.py test` prints "connexion OK" (connection OK) or the HTTP code, then one line per resource of the probe (`accès search : OK`, `accès history : OK`, `accès fields : OK`, or the HTTP code in place of OK: exit code 1), then the token expiry line: `le jeton API expire dans <n> jours` when the date is within the warning delay, `date d'expiration du jeton API dépassée` once past (exit code 1), `date d'expiration du jeton non renseignée` without the setting. Replace the token before the date; an expired or soon-to-expire token is the first thing to check after a 401.
5. **Collect**: `uv run tools/history_jira.py collect [--team "<nom>"]` (no file to give: an export file is refused with exit code 2). The keys are read from the database, in memory. `--team` limits the collection to one team (name given only here, never displayed): do this first, to validate on one team before the whole scope. The `portfolio_types` types and the `excluded_projects` projects of `flux.yaml` are discarded, as in the flow reports. The script uses the bulk history retrieval API if the instance offers it, otherwise the ticket-by-ticket history; it waits with increasing delays, respects `Retry-After` on HTTP 429, and stops on HTTP 401 or 403 (nothing is written then). A `history` run is recorded only when no ticket ended in error.
6. **Read the output**: the number of tickets read, discarded, already collected, to query, processed, the number of status transitions and the errors per HTTP code. Never a key or a name.

**Check the history**: `uv run tools/history_jira.py check [--team "<nom>"]` does not contact Jira and is for you alone in the Terminal (a Claude Code session is refused, exit code 3); it compares the last state in the history of each ticket in the database with the current state of the ticket and prints only counts: consistent tickets, inconsistent, not comparable (no transition), transitions per ticket (0, 1, 2-4, 5-9, 10 and more), delivered tickets with no transition at all. For delivered tickets, the command also gives the origin state of the first known transition (a history truncated at the start shows as an origin state that is not the workflow's initial state), the transitions per ticket, the segments and the states traversed. `--key <clé>` prints the sequence of states of a single ticket with their dates, without author, to compare it with Jira's History tab (Terminal only). If the history looks incomplete, `collect … --refresh` queries all the retained tickets again, ignoring the cache.

The history is in the database (`flow` schema; there is no cache file any more: `Flux-Lab/historique/transitions.json`, if still there, is a file left from the CSV route that `flow_retention.py` counts and deletes). The collection is incremental: a new run queries again only the unfinished tickets, those with no history yet and the finished tickets updated since they were read (the update date kept in the database is compared with the one of the ticket). The report prints `Tickets terminés réinterrogés (mis à jour depuis la dernière collecte) : n` (`Finished tickets queried again (updated since the last collection): n` with `--language en`); the date also moves for a comment or a field change, so a few tickets are queried for no new transition. `--refresh` still queries everything. A ticket in error does not enter it and will be retried. The tests use a fake HTTP server, an in-memory store, invented data and a fake token (the tests on a real database are run by you with `dbtest.sh`): `uv run --with pyyaml --with requests python -m unittest discover -s tests/history_jira -v`. The real calls (`test`, `collect`) are run by you in the Terminal, never by Claude Code: `.claude/settings.json` also forbids it from reading the Keychain (`security find-generic-password`, `security … pmzilla-jira`) and from running these two commands.

### Jira API export

`tools/export_jira_api.py` reads the tickets of a JQL query and writes them straight into the dated raw layer of the database (`flow` schema), in one transaction, under the import role: it writes no file (the CSV route ended with the database-only change; `--output` is refused with exit code 2 and `--full` is accepted and changes nothing, since every ticket is read in full at each run). It is a script separate from `history_jira.py` because it reads other fields (current state, dates, parent, links, team) than the state history alone; it reuses its access to Jira (Keychain token, retries). It asks Jira for no title, no description, no person, no label and no version.

1. **Set `Flux-Lab/flux.yaml`** (in addition to the collection settings): `jql_export` (the query) or `jql_exports` (named queries, `nom: requête JQL`, for example `produit-A` and `produit-B`; takes precedence over `jql_export`), `export_team_field` (the identifier of the "[G] Team/s" field, of the form `customfield_NNNNN`; without it, no team) and, if needed, `export_timezone` (time zone in which the dates are read, `Europe/Paris` by default). `export_team_header`, `export_verification_field` and `export_verification_header` stay accepted and are no longer used.
2. **Find the field identifier** if needed: `uv run tools/export_jira_api.py fields --search team` prints the identifier and name of the fields whose name contains the text (case-insensitive), never a value.
3. **Check the team field, once**: `uv run tools/export_jira_api.py diagnose` queries one page and says the shape of the team field's values (text, list, object and the names of its keys), never a value. If the API does not return a label (Assets object identifier, UUID), the script stops without writing anything unless the referential or the team catalogue holds identifiers (see "Team translation" above and step 4).
4. **Export**: `uv run tools/export_jira_api.py export [--query NOM] [--strict-teams]`. With `jql_export`, one query; with `jql_exports`, without `--query`, all the queries, and `--query NOM` a single one (an unknown name stops the command and lists the available names). Every ticket of every query is read in full at each run, with no condition on the update date. **Raw layer.** The export writes the dated raw layer in the `flow` schema (migration `0009`): one run per successful export (instant, number of named queries read, whether all of them were read), one sighting per ticket read, and one snapshot per ticket that is new or whose content changed (allowed attributes only, with each team's translated name and Jira team identifier, an unknown identifier included; never a title, description, person, label or version), and the team type written into `flow.teams` (migration `0011`; the output prints `équipes sans type : <n>` / `teams without a type: <n>`, a count only). All is written in one transaction: an error from Jira or the database leaves no run. With `--strict-teams`, one unknown team identifier stops the export with exit code 3 and nothing is recorded. The export logs in to the database **before the first Jira call**, as the import role `pmzilla_flow`, with its own password (Keychain service `pmzilla-lab-db-pmzilla_flow`, set once with `uv run tools/db.py role-password pmzilla_flow`); without that password it stops (exit code 1, `mot de passe du rôle d'import absent : le poser avec db.py role-password`), and with an unreachable database too (`base injoignable : export arrêté avant tout appel à Jira`), calling Jira for nothing and recording nothing. `diagnose` and `fields` never connect. The output gives only numbers and ends its counts with `couche brute : <n> tickets vus, <m> instantanés ajoutés` (counts only, never a key or a team).
   **If the API returns only an Assets object identifier** (the diagnostic says so): fill the referential (`jira_team_ids`) and the team catalogue `Flux-Lab/equipes-jira.yaml` (written once by `migrate-teams --from-assets`, see "Team translation" above). `export` translates the identifiers through both; an identifier in neither becomes "(équipe inconnue)" (unknown team), and the Terminal then lists them (identifiers only, never a label or a ticket key) with `identifiers to add`: add each to the referential or to the catalogue by hand. The old `teams-table` command (table built from a manual export) and `compare` (manual export against API) are removed and refused with a message; their `deny` rules in `.claude/settings.json` are kept.

The monthly procedure that chains these commands is described right after.

Tests, on a fake server and invented data: `uv run --with pyyaml --with requests python -m unittest discover -s tests/export_jira_api -v`. The real calls (`fields`, `diagnose`, `export`) are run by you in the Terminal, never by Claude Code: `.claude/settings.json` forbids it.

### Visual flow dashboard

`tools/flow_board.py` produces **a single HTML file**, for the Agile Leads who find Markdown reports hard to read: interactive charts, product view first and then a team selector, a "How to read" on each chart. The file is self-contained and works offline: the charting library (Apache ECharts), the CSS, the JavaScript and the data are embedded in it, with no network call or CDN. It is written in `Flux-Lab/tableaux/` (outside Git, unreadable by Claude Code) from the JSON files in `Flux-Lab/rapports/`. It is **in English by default** (read by English-speaking Agile Leads); the Markdown reports follow the `--language fr|en` option of their script (`flux_jira.py`, `dependencies_jira.py`; French by default, same file names in both languages), and PMZilla's readings stay in French until the commands are translated.

1. **Install the library, once**: `zsh tools/vendor/telecharger_echarts.sh` (version 5.6.0 by default; `zsh tools/vendor/telecharger_echarts.sh 5.6.0 <dossier>` for another folder). The script downloads the npm package, compares its SHA-512 integrity with the registry's, then writes into `tools/vendor/`: `echarts.min.js` (about 1 MB), `LICENSE-echarts` and `NOTICE-echarts` (Apache-2.0), `echarts.sha256` and `echarts.version`. Commit these files: the generator checks the fingerprint at each generation and refuses a modified file. This is the only network call; after that, none.
2. **Set the series** (optional) in `Flux-Lab/flux.yaml`:
   ```yaml
   boards:
     series:
       - name: <série>                  # the --name of flux_jira.py compute
         dependencies: <nom>            # the --name of dependencies_jira.py compute (per team), optional
         project_dependencies: <nom>    # same with --by project, optional
         baseline: "AAAA-MM-JJ"         # date of a pilot's reference report, optional
   ```
   Without this setting, all the series found in `Flux-Lab/rapports/` are displayed (series ending in `…-recent`, and the `…-db` comparison series of the monthly run, are ignored). Series names stay in `Flux-Lab/`.
3. **Produce the Aging WIP points**: add `--points` to the flow `compute` (writes `Flux-Lab/tableaux/points-<série>-AAAA-MM-JJ.json`, one point per in-progress ticket, without key; the reports stay aggregates). The CFD, the time per segment, the delivery lead time and the efficiency require `--history`; the active, waiting and dormant breakdown requires `--dormant-from-history` (or `waiting_states` in `flux.yaml`). A view whose data is missing says so instead of being empty.
4. **Generate**: `uv run tools/flow_board.py` writes `Flux-Lab/tableaux/tableau-flux-AAAA-MM-JJ.html`. Options: `--language en|fr` (`en` by default; the French file carries `-fr`), `--anonymous` (shareable version, see below), `--output <fichier>`, `--open`. Without the library, the command stops and gives the download command.

**The eight views** (each with its "How to read", taken from the shared glossary `tools/flow_glossary.py`, in English and in French): arrivals and departures per week; cumulative flow diagram per segment; time per segment (P50 and P85); Aging WIP (one point per ticket, by state or by segment, with the P85 lead time); WIP breakdown and its evolution from one report to the next; delivery lead time and flow efficiency (with coverage); dependency map as an interactive graph, with its table and the loops; evolution since the pilot's baseline (table of gaps and curves, with the warning when the options of the two reports differ: scope, history, source of dormant tickets). Teams are always in alphabetical order, never ranked.

**Reading and printing.** Okabe-Ito palette colors, readable in light and dark themes (following the system); color never carries information alone: shapes (active ● , waiting ▲, dormant ◇), patterns, labels and values are added to it. The page address keeps the view (`#s=1&t=<équipe>&a=segment&th=dark`: series, team, Aging WIP mode, theme). "Print" prints in A4 landscape: charts at page size, "How to read" unfolded, light theme.

**Anonymous version.** `uv run tools/flow_board.py --anonymous` replaces team, project and series names with "Team A", "Project B", "Series 1" (in French "Équipe", "Projet", "Série") **before** writing anything to the file: no real name is in the page, nor in its data. The mapping is in `Flux-Lab/tableaux/anonymes.yaml` (permissions 600): letters drawn at random the first time, then stable from one month to the next; a new name receives the next free letter. Do not delete this file if an anonymous version has already been shared. Jira state names stay as they are: reread them before sharing.

**Tests** (invented data, temporary folder): `uv run --with pyyaml --with numpy --with requests python -m unittest discover -s tests/flow_board -v`. They run the page's JavaScript under Node in a fake DOM; once the library is installed, they also render each chart with the real ECharts (light, dark, French, English). `Flux-Lab/tableaux/` is forbidden to Claude Code (reading and common commands).

### Monthly run in one command

`tools/flow_monthly.py` chains the monthly commands in a fixed order and stops at the first gap. It adds no calculation: it only passes options (no file path) to the tools below, which read and write the database. **You run it yourself in the Terminal**, from `~/PMZilla-Lab/workspace`: `uv run tools/flow_monthly.py`. Claude Code cannot launch it (`deny` rules, and the script itself refuses to start when the `CLAUDECODE` variable that Claude Code sets in its shells is present). It is not the Claude Code command `/monthly-flow`, which only reads the reports afterwards.

**Once: declare the series.** Add a top-level block `monthly_run` to `Flux-Lab/flux.yaml` (the file stays outside Git; this document gives its structure with generic names only). The export queries are read from the existing `jql_exports` (or `jql_export`) setting, never from the block.

```yaml
monthly_run:
  series:                      # at least one; each item has:
    - name: <série>            #   required: the --name of the reports (letters, digits, - and _), not ending with "-db"
      scope: <clés>            #   optional: the --scope (project keys separated by commas, or "tous"); absent = every project of the database
      dependencies: team       #   optional: team (default), project, both or none
  dashboard: both              # optional: full, anonymous or both (default)
```

Any other key, an empty list, a series without a name, two series with the same name or a name ending with `-db` (a suffix reserved by the former comparison step, still refused) is refused before any step (exit code 3), with the key path and the line number, never a value. The block is ignored by the other tools that read `flux.yaml`.

**The steps, in this order** (identifiers are in English whatever the language; no call carries a file path or `--source`): `export` (`export_jira_api.py export --strict-teams`), `import` (`flow_import.py --mark-absent`, no file), `history` (`history_jira.py collect --strict`, after the import because it reads the tickets the import derived), `flow` (`flux_jira.py compute`, per series, with `--history --dormant-from-history --points`), `dependencies` (`dependencies_jira.py compute`, per series and per configured map), `dashboard` (`flow_board.py`, full and anonymous versions according to `dashboard`) and `retention` (`flow_retention.py purge` as a dry run, never with `--apply`: it deletes nothing, see 14.8). The comparison steps `compare-data` and `compare-reports` no longer exist (`--from compare-data` and `--from compare-reports` are refused, exit code 2): the flow report is computed once per series, from the database. It runs no dry run of the import, no migration or backup (the daily backup job exists), no history check, no segment sample, no ticket inspection.

**Strict options.** Two tools used to exit with 0 while the month was incomplete. `export_jira_api.py export --strict-teams` prints "identifiants d'équipe inconnus : export incomplet" ("unknown team identifiers: export incomplete"), records nothing and exits with 3 when a team identifier is in neither the referential nor the team catalogue (a catalogue team, obsolete or not, never makes the export incomplete). `history_jira.py collect --strict` writes what it collected as it would without the option, then prints "erreurs de collecte : historique incomplet" ("collection errors: history incomplete") and exits with 3 when requests failed (for example under throttling). Without the options both tools behave as before; the orchestrator always passes them.

**Options.**

| Option | Effect |
|---|---|
| `--dry-run` | the same checks as a real run, then one line per call with placeholders (`<série 1>`, `<périmètre 1>`); no tool is run, no summary is written |
| `--from <step>` | runs that step and the following ones, with the run date for the report names; the steps that passed earlier the same day are kept in the summary (a summary written before the database-only change, which lists the removed comparison steps, stays readable: they are ignored). Without `--date`, a resume after `export` reuses the date of the series in progress, read from the summaries of the current month whose `export` step passed, and announces it (`Reprise de la série du AAAA-MM-JJ`), followed by the rewrite warning since that date has a summary (not in a dry run); with several unfinished series, or none, it is refused (exit code 2) and proposes `--date`. It never falls back to today |
| `--date AAAA-MM-JJ` | run date of the report names (resume the next day under the first day's date); a future date is refused, and `--from export` accepts only today. When that date already has a summary, a warning says its reports will be rewritten (no confirmation, no change of the exit code) |
| `--force` | runs even if a summary of the current month is already complete (`--from` does not bypass the refusal) |
| `--verbose` | streams the tools' output after a warning: it can hold series, query or team names |
| `--language fr\|en` | language of the messages and of every tool (French by default) |
| `--open` | opens the dashboards |

**Output and summary.** By default the tools' output is captured in memory and written nowhere; on a failure only the failing tool's error output is printed, then the step, its exit code and the resume command (`Pour reprendre : uv run tools/flow_monthly.py --from <step>`, with `--date` when the run date is not today). Its own lines hold only step identifiers, series ranks (`série 1`), counts, exit codes and durations. Each run also writes `Flux-Lab/rapports/suivi-mensuel-AAAA-MM-JJ.md` (permissions 600): per step and series rank, `passed`, `failed`, `skipped` or `not run` with the exit code and the duration (`gap`, the former parity gap, can still appear in an old summary), the number of report files of the run date, and the final status (`complete` or `stopped at <step>`). Claude Code may read this folder, so `/monthly-flow` can use the summary.

**Exit codes.** 0 complete; 1 a step failed (any non-zero exit code of a tool); 2 usage error (an unknown step, the removed `compare-data` and `compare-reports` included); 3 prerequisite refused (settings file, export query or `monthly_run` block missing or invalid, run under Claude Code); 5 a summary of the current month is already complete (`--force` to run again). The codes 4 (parity gap between the database and the CSV) and 6 (missing input) are retired and never reused. The orchestrator never tests a file itself.

**What stays manual.** Completing the referential or `Flux-Lab/equipes-jira.yaml` when the export lists identifiers to add (then `--from export`), the reading in Claude Code (`/monthly-flow <série>`, `/dependencies <série>`; the orchestrator prints these commands at the end), and the history check from time to time (step 9 below). The step-by-step procedure that follows stays the reference to run one step by hand.

**Tests**, with fake tools and invented settings (no Jira, no database): `uv run --with pyyaml python -W ignore -m unittest discover -s tests/flow_monthly -q`. They also check that the script opens nothing but the settings file and the reports folder, and that nothing it prints or writes carries a series, query or scope name.

### Monthly procedure through the API

The steps below are the reference to run one step by hand; the whole sequence is one command (see "Monthly run in one command" just above).
