11. **MCP smoke check.** In a new Claude Code session, `/mcp` lists seven tools for the library server; one `search_library` call returns passages; one `list_due_reviews` call returns a list or a configured-author error, never a backend error.

**Rollback of the switch-over**, only if step 10 or 11 fails because of a missing file: copy the files back from the labelled folder (`cp -p "$D"/*.sqlite* ~/.pmzilla-lab/`, with `D=~/.pmzilla-lab/conservation/db-2026-10-14-sortiesqlite` for the switch-over of 2026-10-14), check them with `(cd "$D" && shasum -a 256 -c SHA256SUMS)`, and report the failing step before anything else.

The variable `D` lives only in the Terminal window of step 7: in a new window, set it again with the date of step 6. Once step 11 has passed, move the labelled folder out of `sauvegardes/` into `~/.pmzilla-lab/conservation/`, out of reach of any cleaning of the backup folder (`mkdir -p ~/.pmzilla-lab/conservation && mv "$D" ~/.pmzilla-lab/conservation/`), and set `D` to the new path. For the switch-over of 2026-10-14, the folder is `~/.pmzilla-lab/conservation/db-2026-10-14-sortiesqlite` (the copies, the dumps, `SHA256SUMS`, `parite-sqlite` and `parite-sqlite.log`).

**Retention.** The labelled folder `db-AAAA-MM-JJ-sortiesqlite`, in `~/.pmzilla-lab/conservation/`, is kept 90 days (for the switch-over of 2026-10-14: `~/.pmzilla-lab/conservation/db-2026-10-14-sortiesqlite`, until 2027-01-12, idea #209 of the work queue), then you delete it by hand, provided that every daily run of the period ended without a failure tied to the former files and that one restore test (`tools/db_restore_test.sh`, section 14.2) succeeded after the deletion. Note the deletion date in the work queue.

**Restore the library from a labelled backup** (you alone, in your Terminal, from `~/PMZilla-Lab/workspace`; it replaces the whole schema `knowledge` of the real database by the one of the backup, and touches neither `cases` nor `flow`):

1. **Stop both watch jobs** for the duration, so that no indexing writes during the restore (the catch-up job would start a run within the hour), and close the Claude Code sessions, so that no search runs:
   ```bash
   launchctl bootout gui/$(id -u)/com.pmzilla.watch
   launchctl bootout gui/$(id -u)/com.pmzilla.watch-catchup
   ```
   If a run is in progress (`ls ~/.pmzilla-lab/veille.verrou`), wait for its end first.
2. **Check the folder** (labelled folders are named `db-AAAA-MM-JJ-<label>`, outside the rotation):
   ```bash
   ls -l ~/.pmzilla-lab/sauvegardes/db-AAAA-MM-JJ-<label>/knowledge.dump
   ```
3. **Restore the `knowledge` dump** into the real database, in one transaction that stops at the first error (read `pg_restore --help` first if in doubt):
   ```bash
   docker compose exec -T db pg_restore -U postgres -d pmzilla --clean --if-exists --single-transaction --exit-on-error < ~/.pmzilla-lab/sauvegardes/db-AAAA-MM-JJ-<label>/knowledge.dump
   ```
4. **Check**: `uv run ~/PMZilla-Lab/pmzilla_lab_library.py status` (the counts of the backup day) and `uv run ~/PMZilla-Lab/pmzilla_lab_library.py probe` (`BM25 index readable`).
5. **Bring the library up to date**: `uv run ~/PMZilla-Lab/pmzilla_lab_library.py index ~/PMZilla-Lab/workspace/Bibliotheque-Lab ~/PMZilla-Lab/workspace/Watch-Lab` re-embeds only what was collected or changed since the backup.
6. **Start the watch jobs again**:
   ```bash
   launchctl bootstrap gui/$(id -u) ~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.pmzilla.watch.plist
   launchctl bootstrap gui/$(id -u) ~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.pmzilla.watch-catchup.plist
   ```
   Loading the catch-up job starts it once: if today's run has not ended, it runs now.

A backup older than a migration may not restore onto the migrated database: `pg_restore` then fails and, with `--single-transaction`, leaves the schema as it was; take a more recent labelled backup.

### 14.4 The cases module (lot 2)

The cases module keeps your experiments and, once reviewed, your cases (what you tried, the result, what got stuck) in the schema `cases`, and finds the closest ones when you submit a problem. The MCP server stays read-only: it offers three tools (`search_cases`, `search_experiments`, `list_due_reviews`), so the server now has seven tools. Everything is written through `tools/journal_lab.py experiment …` (modes of `/journal`: `start`, `note`, `show`, `postpone`, `drop`, `close`, `due`), under the role `pmzilla_cases`. Run the steps in this order, in a Terminal:

1. **Tests with the database** (each must end with `OK` and no `skipped=`):
   ```bash
   for suite in db hybrid_search cases_store library_backend; do PMZILLA_LAB_DB_PASSWORD="$(security find-generic-password -s pmzilla-lab-db -w)" uv run --with numpy --with httpx --with pyyaml --with "psycopg[binary]" python -m unittest discover -s tests/$suite 2>&1 | tail -3; done
   ```
2. **Back up (label made of letters only), then migrate** (migration `0006_cases_experiments.sql`: three tables, BM25 and HNSW indexes, no data):
   ```bash
   DB_BACKUP_DATE=$(date +%Y-%m-%d)-avantlotdeux bash tools/db_backup.sh
   uv run tools/db.py migrate --dry-run
   uv run tools/db.py migrate
   ```
3. **Two roles that log in**, each with its own generated password, stored in the Keychain and tested by the command (nothing to type or paste):
   ```bash
   uv run tools/db.py role-password pmzilla_cases_ro
   uv run tools/db.py role-password pmzilla_cases
   ```
4. **Your author identifier** (local, never in a tracked file). Note the total of the personal-identifiers scan, write the identifier, check that the file is ignored by Git, then check that the total did not change:
   ```bash
   python3 tools/identifiants_personnels.py | tail -1
   printf 'author: %s\n' "<your-identifier>" > .pmzilla/cases.yaml
   git check-ignore .pmzilla/cases.yaml
   python3 tools/identifiants_personnels.py | tail -1
   ```
   If the second total is higher, rerun with `--lignes` and remove the occurrences before going on.
5. **Checks:** `uv run tools/db.py status` (0006 applied, nothing pending); `uv run tools/journal_lab.py experiment due` (no review due, exit code 0); `uv run tools/dashboard.py --open` (the seventh section says "no data yet"); `claude mcp get pmzilla-library` (connected), then `/mcp` in a new Claude Code session shows seven tools; `test -f Flux-Lab/noms-sensibles.txt && echo present` prints `present` (without a list of sensitive names, or with an empty one, every experiment write is refused).
6. **Session hook.** `.claude/settings.json` declares a `SessionStart` hook that lists the reviews that are due, without running anything: `uv run tools/journal_lab.py experiment due --session-hook`. A new session shows no error from the hook and, until a review is due, no line. Evaluation sessions run the hook on the disposable database; no shipped case has a review due, and without a disposable database (empty author, no `.pmzilla/cases.yaml` in the copy) the hook prints nothing and exits 0, so it never changes a graded answer.
7. **Evaluation set**, French then English (each run is paid; 37 of 37 expected): `uv run tools/posture_evals.py` and `uv run tools/posture_evals.py --language en`. The cases run against a disposable cases database filled with invented data, never your real cases. The three cases that use a cases fixture (`cas-proche-reussi`, `cas-proche-echoue`, `experimentation-en-cours`) need that disposable database: the PostgreSQL instance must be running (`bash tools/db_up.sh`) and the superuser password must be reachable as for `tools/db.py`, in the Keychain (service `pmzilla-lab-db`) or, as a fallback, in the variable `PMZILLA_LAB_DB_PASSWORD`; the script declares its own dependencies (`psycopg[binary]`, `httpx`, `numpy`, `mcp`), so a plain `uv run` is enough and no variable is needed when the Keychain holds the password. If the database cannot be created, the first line of the run says why (the message names the Keychain service and the variable, never a value), those cases are reported as "non évaluable ici : cases database unavailable", the summary reads `BILAN 34/37 · 3 non évaluable(s) · passage non validant` and the exit code is 3: such a run validates nothing, whatever its score. To replay only those three cases, in French then in English:
   ```bash
   uv run tools/posture_evals.py --cases cas-proche-reussi,cas-proche-echoue,experimentation-en-cours
   uv run tools/posture_evals.py --language en --cases cas-proche-reussi,cas-proche-echoue,experimentation-en-cours
   ```
   A partial run is never a reference; the acceptance of 37 of 37 is read on the two complete runs.

The similarity threshold of the search (`MIN_SIMILARITY` in `tools/cases_store.py`) is provisional: it was calibrated on the invented set of the evaluation cases (`uv run tools/cases_calibration.py`) and is recalibrated after your first ten real cases. The `experience` block of the journal is deprecated: it is still read, with a warning, and a later change removes it.

### 14.5 Suites with a throwaway database

The suites with database tests refuse the database of `bash tools/db_up.sh` (port 5432, the real one, or the port configured for it): they need a throwaway PostgreSQL instance on ANOTHER port, in which they create their databases (`pmzilla_test_<hex>`, dropped after each test). The mark is the variable `DBTEST_PORT`, set by `tools/dbtest.sh` alone to the port of its container: a test uses a database only when that mark and the connection port (`PMZILLA_LAB_DB_PORT`) are both written in the plain decimal form (ASCII digits only, no sign, no space, no leading zero, from 1 to 65535: `05432`, `+55432` or `65536` are refused), the mark is not 5432 and equals the connection port as a number, the address variable, if any, names the same port, and a password is set; it makes no connection attempt otherwise. Without such an instance the tests that need it are skipped, with the message « base jetable absente (lancer bash tools/dbtest.sh) ». A complete `bash tests/run_all.sh` removes every `PMZILLA_LAB_DB_*` variable (address, port, passwords, Keychain override) from the environment of the suites, so a password exported in your terminal never reaches them; it says so on one line before the first suite.

`tools/dbtest.sh` does it all. You type it in your Terminal, Claude Code is not allowed to run it (`deny` rules, like `db_up.sh`):

```bash
cd ~/PMZilla-Lab/workspace
bash tools/dbtest.sh                          # db, flow_import, flow_store, flow_views, hybrid_search, pmzilla_lab_library, library_backend, cases_store, watch_store, book_usage, dashboard, flow_retention, posture_evals, export_jira_api, history_jira, story_quality
bash tools/dbtest.sh flow_store flow_import   # only these suites (folders of tests/)
bash tools/dbtest.sh --port 55433             # another host port (default 55432; never 5432 nor the real database's port)
```

- It starts the container `pmzilla-lab-test-db` (the image pinned in `docker-compose.yml`, published on `127.0.0.1` only) with a random password. The password lives in the script only: it is never displayed, never written, never exported in your shell, and the Keychain password of the real database is not read.
- It refuses to start if the port is 5432 (or the real database's port, compared as numbers), if `--port` is not in the plain decimal form (`Port invalide`, exit 2), if the configured real-database port (`PMZILLA_LAB_DB_PORT`) is set and unreadable, i.e. not in that form (exit 2, nothing started; `tools/db_rehearsal.sh` and `tools/db_restore_test.sh` apply the same rule to their `--port` and to that variable), if a suite does not exist, or if a container named `pmzilla-lab-test-db` already exists (it is then neither touched nor removed).
- It runs each suite with `bash tests/run_all.sh <suite>` and no pipe, with no database variable of your shell (the password of a role, the Keychain override and the address are removed) but its own port, its own password and `DBTEST_PORT`, then prints « Suites vertes » and « Suites en échec ». The exit code is 1 if a suite fails or the container does not start, 2 for a refusal.
- **The record of a green run.** A complete green run of the default list leaves a proof that finishing a branch requires when the branch changes database code (see "Finishing a branch", code 14). The script removes from the file `pmzilla-dbtest-ok` of the common Git folder (shared by the workspace and its worktrees) the line of the tree of `HEAD`, runs the suites, and appends that tree (one line, a tree identifier, no branch, path, date or password; owner-only file, written through a temporary file) only if all hold: no suite was named (`--port` is allowed), every suite is green, the working tree was clean at the start and at the end (untracked files that are not ignored count), the tree of `HEAD` did not change during the run, and the published port of the container answered from the Mac before the suites (otherwise every database test would be skipped and the run green for nothing: the line is then `Empreinte dbtest non écrite (base injoignable depuis l'hôte)`, exit code unchanged). Otherwise it writes nothing and says why in one line (`suites choisies`, `arbre non propre`, `arbre changé pendant le passage`, `hors dépôt Git`, `écriture impossible`); after a red run it says nothing. The proof is exact: a later commit, or a merge of `main`, needs a new run. The exit code never changes because of the record. It is a declaration of your own run, like the evaluation records: a hand-written line would pass.
- It removes the container and its volume (`docker rm -f -v`) at the end, also after a failure or Ctrl+C. If it says « ATTENTION … existe encore », run the `docker rm -f -v pmzilla-lab-test-db` command it prints.
- The suites apply the migrations themselves in each test database. A suite whose tests need the database must no longer show them as skipped (`skipped=…` in its line); the two diagnostics of `hybrid_search` (`PMZILLA_DIAG=1` shows them) stay skipped, as expected.

### 14.6 Damaged BM25 index

**Symptom.** A text search on the PostgreSQL route fails with `psycopg.errors.InternalError_: bitmap intersection stream (consumer 0, segment …) claimed twice` (pg_search, ParadeDB 0.26.0), typically on queries filtered by passage type (`types: ["figure"]`); the vector component and unfiltered text queries may still work. The error always names the same segment: the index `knowledge.chunks_bm25` is damaged (a disposable database, with a fresh index, does not show it). Setting `enable_bitmapscan = off` does not help.

**Detection.** The daily watch run checks it: after a successful indexing, `watch.sh` runs `uv run ~/PMZilla-Lab/pmzilla_lab_library.py probe`, which reads the BM25 index twice (without and with the passage-type filter) under the read-only role, writes nothing and prints no text of a book. On failure the probe prints `BM25 index unreadable ({variant}: {error type})` and `Procedure: docs/pmzilla/installation.md, "Damaged BM25 index"`, and exits with code 3 (2: database unreachable); the watch log then says `Index BM25 illisible` and a macOS notification points here. Any other non-zero code gives `Sonde de l'index BM25 en échec (code {n}) : voir ci-dessus` and the notification `Sonde de l'index BM25 en échec. Voir le journal de la veille.` The watch itself is never blocked, the probe is never a failed step and the exit code of the run does not change. Nothing is repaired automatically.

**Rebuild** (you alone, as superuser, in your Terminal; Claude Code does not run it):

```bash
# psql as the superuser of the cluster, connected to the library database; adapt the connection to your setup
SET max_parallel_maintenance_workers = 0;
REINDEX INDEX knowledge.chunks_bm25;
```

The operation rebuilds the index only: no passage, vector or book is touched or lost. It reads the table and rewrites the index, so allow a few minutes on the whole library, and run it while no search is running.

**Check.** `uv run ~/PMZilla-Lab/pmzilla_lab_library.py probe` must print `BM25 index readable` (exit code 0), then `uv run tools/library_parity.py` must exit 0 against the recorded snapshot (section 14.3, "Snapshot of the search results"): the probe says the index is readable, the comparison says it ranks as before. There is no fallback route: while the index is damaged, a database error in a search tool answers an error object that points to `bash tools/db_up.sh` and to this section. Re-record the snapshot only if the comparison reports a difference you have explained (books added, for example), never to hide one.

### 14.7 Off-site backup (encrypted external disk)

Every backup of 14.2 lives on the Mac itself, next to the data it protects: a failed internal disk, a stolen Mac or a `colima delete` followed by a disk failure loses both. The sanctums (`_bmad/memory/`) are no longer in Git, so their backups are their only copy. `tools/offsite_backup.sh` copies the newest backups to **one** encrypted external disk, with a checksum manifest, and tests the restore of that copy every month.

> **Warning: keep the disk password outside the Mac as well** (a password manager, or on paper kept elsewhere). macOS may remember it in your login Keychain so that the disk unlocks when plugged in, but if the Mac is lost with the only copy of the password, the off-site copy is lost with it. The script never reads, asks for, stores or prints a password and never calls the Keychain.

**Nothing leaves for an online service.** The script has no network command and refuses a destination inside a cloud-synced folder (iCloud Drive, Dropbox, OneDrive and similar), a network volume and a Git work tree. A test of the suite `tests/offsite_backup` scans its source for network commands.

**What is copied, and what is not.** For the database (`db-AAAA-MM-JJ`) and for the sanctums (`bmad-memory-AAAA-MM-JJ`), the newest *plain* dated backup of the local backups folder, if the disk does not hold it yet. Not copied: backups with a label (`db-AAAA-MM-JJ-matin`, restore points before an operation), the other folders of the backups folder, and the other personal data (library, watch, journal, local context, flow folder; the migration bundle of `install-migrate` covers them). The sanctum backup that the local rotation never deletes is copied once, on request only: `copy --include-protected`. The sanctum backup itself is still made by hand (`bash tools/sauvegarde_sanctum.sh`): the off-site copy is only as recent as the last manual run.

**One-time setup (in a regular Terminal).**

1. Format the disk as **APFS (Encrypted)** in Disk Utility, choose a password and store it outside the Mac (warning above). Plug it in and unlock it.
2. Record it: `bash tools/offsite_backup.sh setup /Volumes/<disk name>`. The script checks that the volume is encrypted, external, local and outside a synced folder or Git work tree, shows the volume name and asks `y/N`. On `y`, it records the **volume identifier** (not the name) in `~/.pmzilla-lab/offsite/configuration` (owner-only) and creates the folder `pmzilla-offsite` at the volume root. From then on the disk is found by its identifier only: another disk with the same name receives nothing.
3. First copy, then first test, by hand: `bash tools/offsite_backup.sh copy`, then `bash tools/offsite_backup.sh restore-test --force` (add `--detail` to see the `pg_restore` errors on screen; they are never written to the report). Expected: one copy per series on the disk, `test de restauration hors du Mac : OK`, and a report in `~/.pmzilla-lab/offsite/rapports/` (the real database must be running for the test: `bash tools/db_up.sh`).

**Copy.** Each copy is written into `.partiel-<name>` with a manifest (SHA-256 and size of every file), verified on the disk, then renamed: a half-written copy is never taken for a complete one, and a leftover temporary folder is removed at the next run. Folders are `700` and files `600`. The run is idempotent (`déjà présente : <name>`). Not enough free space gives `place insuffisante sur le disque` (exit 1) and nothing is written. After a verified copy, a rotation keeps in each series the 7 newest copies plus the newest copy of each of the 12 most recent months (`OFFSITE_GARDER_RECENTES`, `OFFSITE_GARDER_MOIS`, integers of at least 1, otherwise exit 2 and nothing is touched); it deletes only folders it wrote, one `copie supprimée : <name>` line each, and never the newest copy.

**Absent or locked disk.** The run is deferred: `disque absent ou verrouillé : copie reportée`, exit 3, nothing written, no notification while the last successful copy is less than 7 days old (`OFFSITE_AGE_MAX_JOURS`). Older, or never: one macOS notification per day, `aucune copie hors du Mac depuis <n> jours`. A refusal (unencrypted volume, internal disk, network volume, synced folder or Git work tree: exit 2) and a failure (exit 1) notify under the scheduler; a run by hand never notifies. Exit codes: 0 done, already present or test not due; 1 failure; 2 usage or environment refused; 3 deferred.

**Monthly restore test.** `restore-test` works only when no successful test exists for the current month (`test du mois déjà fait` otherwise; `--force` skips the check). It (1) checks the manifest of the newest copy of each series (every file present, same size and checksum, nothing extra); (2) restores the newest off-site database copy through `tools/db_restore_test.sh --backup <copy>` into a throwaway container, never into the real database, which is read in counts only; (3) compares the file count of the newest sanctum copy with its manifest. The real database must be running, otherwise the test is deferred (`vraie base indisponible : test reporté`, exit 3): the script never starts it. This test complements the quarterly local test of 14.2 (b): it proves that the copy kept away from the Mac restores.

**Report and status.** One report per month, `~/.pmzilla-lab/offsite/rapports/restore-AAAA-MM.txt` (owner-only, the 12 newest kept), holding only counts: run date, copy names (dates), checksum result per series, table names with their two counts and status (a table the copy does not contain shows `MANQUE`, with `-` as its restored count), the failure lines of the restore (`ÉCHEC …`, table names and counts only), sanctum file counts, result and exit code. When migrations present in the files and in the real database are absent from the tested copy, one line says `sauvegarde antérieure à la dernière migration appliquée : migrations absentes de la copie : 7, 8` (numbers only): the copy predates those migrations, so take a newer local backup, copy it, and run `restore-test --force` again. `bash tools/offsite_backup.sh status` prints the status file (last success and last result of the copy and of the test, newest copy of each series) and its age, without mounting or writing anything. Nothing is written in the repository.

**Scheduling (optional, you alone).** Two templates, like the database backup: `tools/com.pmzilla.offsite-copy.plist` (every day at 8:15, after the 7:45 backup, and at every volume mount) and `tools/com.pmzilla.offsite-restore-test.plist` (every day at 8:45 and at every volume mount; the subcommand works only when the test is due). Neither runs at load, neither keeps alive. Install and load them in a regular Terminal:

```bash
for job in offsite-copy offsite-restore-test; do
  sed -e "s|__HOME__|$HOME|g" -e "s|__LAB__|$HOME/PMZilla-Lab|g" ~/PMZilla-Lab/workspace/tools/com.pmzilla.$job.plist > ~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.pmzilla.$job.plist
  xattr -c ~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.pmzilla.$job.plist
  launchctl print gui/$(id -u)/com.pmzilla.$job >/dev/null 2>&1 || launchctl bootstrap gui/$(id -u) ~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.pmzilla.$job.plist
done
```

Logs: `~/Library/Logs/pmzilla-offsite-copy.log` and `~/Library/Logs/pmzilla-offsite-restore-test.log`. To uninstall: `launchctl bootout gui/$(id -u)/com.pmzilla.offsite-copy` (and `...offsite-restore-test`), then remove the two files from `~/Library/LaunchAgents/`. Claude Code is not allowed to run `launchctl` on these jobs, nor the script, nor to read the `pmzilla-offsite` folder: `.claude/settings.json` denies them (`launchctl` on the `com.pmzilla.offsite-*` labels, the four subcommands of the script, any read or listing of `pmzilla-offsite`). The reports and the status file stay readable to it, since they hold counts only.

**Limits.** The copy is in clear text while the disk is mounted (the encryption protects the disk at rest): unplug it when you do not need it, which is also why the copy job waits for the next mount. A Python one-liner is not blocked by the deny rules; the rule of conduct and the counts-only output remain the guard. Properties of the disk utility (`diskutil info -plist`) that tell "encrypted", "internal", "network" and "locked" apart are read in one place of the script and can be overridden by environment variables (`OFFSITE_CLES_CHIFFRE`, `OFFSITE_CLE_INTERNE`, `OFFSITE_CLE_RESEAU`, `OFFSITE_CLE_VERROUILLE`, see the header of the script); confirm them on your macOS version at the first `setup`.

### 14.8 Retention of flow data (one-year rule)

Flow data about tickets (keys, one row per ticket) are kept at most one year. `tools/flow_retention.py` counts and deletes what is past the rule; **you alone run it, in the Terminal**: it refuses to start in a Claude Code session (exit code 3). The clock of a ticket is its resolution date when it is resolved, otherwise the date it left the export; an open ticket still in the export is never past the rule. The rule applies to the ageing-points files, the database and the labelled backups; the files left from the CSV route (every CSV file of the exports folder and the former history cache file) are out of rule whatever their age, and `purge --apply` deletes them (database-only change). Aggregated reports that hold no ticket key and no person (flow and dependency reports, monthly summaries, epic statuses and cycles reports) and the dashboard pages `Flux-Lab/tableaux/tableau-flux-*.html` stay outside the rule: the tool neither counts nor deletes them, whatever their age.

Settings, in the `retention` block of `flux.yaml` (all optional; periods in days):

```yaml
retention:
  period_days: 365            # retention period, refused above one year
  max_file_age_days: 365      # maximum age of an ageing-points file (the name is unchanged; it no longer applies to CSV files); never above period_days
  labelled_backup_days: 30    # life of the flow dump of a labelled backup
  offsite_flow_age_days: 35   # age from which an off-site copy loses its flow dump
  backup_label: rgpd          # lowercase letters and digits only
```

1. **Check, deletes nothing**: `uv run tools/flow_retention.py check [--language fr|en]` prints counts per store and a total line: `contrôle de conservation : rien hors règle` (exit code 0) or `contrôle de conservation : <n> éléments hors règle` (exit code 4). It counts, on one line, the files left from the CSV route (`fichiers laissés par la voie CSV : <n>` / `files left from the CSV route: <n>`): every CSV file of the exports folder and the former history cache file, whatever their age, without reading them (no header check any more). Output: store names, counts and ages in days, never a key, a file name or a value.
2. **Dry run**: `uv run tools/flow_retention.py purge` (without `--apply`) prints the same counts, then `essai : rien n'est supprimé`, and exits 0 whatever it finds.
3. **Labelled backup of the day, then the real purge.** The purge needs a backup of the day carrying the `backup_label` label; without it, it stops with exit code 3 (`sauvegarde avant purge absente : lancer d'abord la sauvegarde étiquetée`):
   ```bash
   DB_BACKUP_DATE=$(date +%Y-%m-%d)-rgpd bash tools/db_backup.sh
   uv run tools/flow_retention.py purge --apply
   ```
   The backup creates `db-AAAA-MM-JJ-rgpd`, which the rotation of `db_backup.sh` ignores (a labelled folder). The purge then deletes, in this order: the files left from the CSV route (the CSV files and the whole former history cache file), the points files past the rule, the tickets of the database in one transaction (cancelled on failure; their teams, transitions, links, label and version periods go with them), and the flow dump of the labelled backups older than `labelled_backup_days` (their knowledge and cases dumps stay). It prints `purge faite`. One labelled backup per purge, so that at most one lifetime of flow data stays in labelled backups.
4. **Off-site copies.** `tools/offsite_backup.sh copy` removes the flow dump from the database copies older than `OFFSITE_FLOW_AGE_DAYS` days (35 by default, integer of at least 1; the date of a copy is the one in its name), rewrites their manifest and verifies it, then prints `flux retiré : <copie>` per copy; the knowledge and cases dumps and the sanctum copies are never touched, and the flow dump of the newest complete copy is never removed. Keep the value a little above the purge interval (the monthly cadence), so that no copy holds flow data that the database no longer holds. The script records in its status file the number of copies still holding an old flow dump, which `check` reads.
5. **Monthly run.** `uv run tools/flow_monthly.py` ends with a `retention` step that runs `purge` as a dry run, never with `--apply`, and prints `essai de purge : <n> éléments hors règle` (the total also goes into the summary). Out-of-rule counts never stop the run; a tool error stops it at `retention` (exit code 1, resume with `--from retention`). The real purge stays the command of step 3.
   **Raw layer.** `check` and `purge` add the line `couche brute : <n> instantanés hors règle`. Out of the rule: the snapshots (and sightings) of a ticket past the one-year rule, which leave in the same transaction as the ticket's rows and before them, and the snapshots and sightings older than `max_file_age_days` (the raw layer keeps the age rule that was the CSV files' and now applies to the points files too), except the latest snapshot and the last sighting of each ticket. Runs hold no ticket data and stay. The line counts snapshots only.
6. **Export and import.** `export_jira_api.py export` writes no row of a ticket already past the rule (`tickets au-delà de la conservation, non écrits : <n>`); `history_jira.py collect` and `flow_import.py` skip such tickets (`tickets au-delà de la conservation, ignorés : <n>`).

**One-off deletion of the CSV files left (you alone; database-only change).** In order: (1) before the merge of the removal, the last comparison on the old code (`flow_compare.py reports` on a CSV report and a database report of the same day, exit 0); (2) a labelled backup, then the restore test on that backup folder named explicitly (`restore OK`); (3) the merge; (4) `flow_retention.py purge` (dry run: the line `files left from the CSV route: <n>`), then `purge --apply` after the labelled backup of the day; (5) `flow_retention.py check` exits 0. The recurring purge afterwards is backup, purge, check, with no export-file store.

Tests, on invented data and throwaway folders (the parts that need the database are skipped without Docker): `bash tests/run_all.sh flow_retention`.

## 15. Repository audit (`/audit`)

The `/audit` command (`.claude/commands/audit.md`) checks the repository without changing anything: it makes no edit and no commit, runs no command on the database, no test suite and no evaluation set. It writes only `.pmzilla/audit/YYYY-MM-DD.md` (the report) and `.pmzilla/audit/dernier-commit` (the hash of `HEAD` at the end of the pass), both outside Git.

- `/audit` (default, Sonnet, about 5 minutes, for the end of a session): state for the night (branch, status, unpushed commits, unmerged branches, stash; first, the run tree read with `bash tools/run_worktree.sh status`: missing, behind `main`, on a branch, off history or with local changes are findings whose action is for you; the development checkout's branch is no longer a risk for the night), OpenSpec changes not archived, documentation for the files changed since `dernier-commit` and any change to the frozen CHANGELOG, books added without a complete entry in `tools/book_catalog.yaml`, the identifier scanner on the changed files, backup or temporary files left (by name only).
- `/audit complet` (Opus, parallel agents by domain: documentation, code, tests, configuration): the full audit, with four tables (to correct, to complete, to delete, to keep despite appearances) and a cleanup plan in thematic commits.

Each finding is numbered with an action, a proof and a risk; what an OpenSpec change in progress already covers is reported as covered, not as a finding. The message ends by asking which numbers to handle: nothing is handled before the answer. Create `.pmzilla/audit/` once (`mkdir -p .pmzilla/audit`) if it does not exist.

## 16. Working on the repository

### Finishing a branch (`tools/finish_branch.sh`)

The script merges the current work branch into `main`, pushes `main` and deletes the branch. Run it in your own Terminal, from the repository, on the branch to finish: `bash tools/finish_branch.sh`. Claude Code cannot launch it (deny rules cover every launcher and path form); reading it and running its test suite (`bash tests/finish_branch/test_finish_branch.sh`) stay allowed. It is already covered by the `chmod +x tools/*.sh` line of section 3.

**Rules for a Claude Code session.** No session pushes or merges: `git push` in every form (a work branch and `--dry-run` included), `--force` and `--no-verify` on a git command, `git merge` in the main workspace and `gh pr merge` are denied; the finishing scripts you launch in your Terminal outside any session do push and merge. The bare `git merge` rules live only in the main workspace's `.claude/settings.local.json`: merging `main` into a work branch is done from a session opened inside the worktree, or by you. The deny rules also refuse writes inside a `.git` folder (the main workspace's `.git/`, a worktree's `.git` file, the common git dir: hooks, fingerprints, the finish lock, config) with the editing tools and with the usual Bash writers, `.git` quoted or not; ordinary git commands and reading stay allowed. Limits: a script, `sh -c`, a one-liner or `cd .git && …` is not refused by a rule; the instruction "never write inside a `.git` folder, even after a git command was refused" covers them. Effects: the five redirection rules refuse any command where a `>` (a redirection, `2>&1`, a quoted `->`) comes before `.git/`, a word ending in `.git`, `.git ` or `.git` followed by a quote, so for a read write the command without `>` (`git status |& grep .git/`, or without `2>&1`); no narrower rule can be written without reopening a write. A commit message that quotes such a write (for example `rm -rf .git`) is refused too: write the message without that text.

It checks, in this order, and stops at the first refusal before touching any branch: usage, a branch to finish (not `main`, not a detached head), a clean working tree, every linked OpenSpec change archived (a branch that changes only `openspec/` is treated as a specification branch), a recorded validating evaluation run when `CLAUDE.md`, the posture skills, the Critic agent, the posture vocabulary or the frozen posture memory changed, `main` up to date with the remote, the branch containing `main`, then a complete green run of `bash tests/run_all.sh`. A branch that changes database code (the migrations and their views, `docker-compose.yml`, `tools/db*` and `tools/flow_*`, the tool modules and the test files of the suites with a database, the tools that import or launch the database such as the calibration and morning-summary tools, the off-site copy script, the backup plist and the database steps of the installer, the list `CHEMINS_BASE` in the script, kept honest by a static test in `tests/db` that checks both the test files and the tools) is refused with code 14 unless the record of a green `bash tools/dbtest.sh` run exists for the tree of its tip (see section 14.5); the check comes after the evaluation-run check and before the check on `main`, and no variable waives it. After the push and the deletion of the branch it runs `tools/run_worktree.sh update` and prints a reminder that the library server keeps the old code until the next session; with no run tree it says so and exits 0. It never forces, rebases, resolves a conflict, bypasses a hook or runs the evaluation set.

| Code | Meaning |
|---|---|
| 0 | merged, pushed, branch deleted |
| 1 | complete run red, nothing merged |
| 2 | usage |
| 3 | no branch to finish |
| 4 | working tree not clean |
| 5 | OpenSpec change not archived |
| 6 | validating evaluation run missing (or its tested state no longer exists: run the set again on the committed state) |
| 7 | `main` not up to date |
| 8 | the branch does not contain `main` |
| 9 | merge impossible, aborted, back on the branch |
| 10 | push refused: the merge exists on local `main` only; the message gives the retry and undo commands |
| 11 | branch not deleted after a successful merge and push |
| 12 | `main` is open in another folder (a detached run tree does not raise it) |
| 13 | merged and pushed, but the run tree is not updated (see "Run worktree"): the message gives the cause and `bash tools/run_worktree.sh update` |
| 14 | the branch changes database code and no green `bash tools/dbtest.sh` run is recorded for the tree of its tip (nothing changed; run it in the branch's worktree, clean tree, after the last commit, then run again) |
| 15 | another branch finish is running (the finish lock, see "Several sessions"): refused at once, nothing is changed or waited for; checked right after the usage check, before code 3 |
| 17 | the morning guard refuses (see "The morning guard of finishes"): inside the morning window and the morning state is not verified; nothing is changed; checked right after the lock (15), before code 3, never in `--check` mode |
| 18 | light path of a specification branch: the strict validation of the touched OpenSpec items (`tools/light_validate.py`) finds a new strict finding, nothing merged (run at launch, after the check of code 12 and before the runner; never in `--check` mode). Code 16 stays reserved |
| 19 | the branch touches an OpenSpec change other than its own (`la branche touche un autre change OpenSpec : <changes>`): a branch carries one change; move that work to its own branch. A specification branch and a train branch `lot/*` are exempt (the train prints `branche de train : changes portés : <changes>`), and the archive folder of another change is allowed with an information line. Checked after code 4 and before code 5, also in `--check` mode (change `finish-under-lock`) |

With code 8 the script also prints a preview of the merge of the most advanced `main` into the branch tip (`fusion de main prévue : sans conflit`, or `conflit sur <n> fichier(s)` and the paths), in both modes; the preview changes no reference, index or file, and the code stays 8.

`bash tools/finish_branch.sh --check <branch>` runs only the refusals 3, 4, 19, 5, 6, 14, 7, 8 and 12 for the named branch, in that order, with the same codes and phrases, and exits 0 with `finish_branch : vérification seule : la branche <branch> peut être terminée (le runner complet reste à lancer)`. It merges, pushes, deletes and fast-forwards nothing (a local `main` behind the remote is accepted and not moved: it only fetches), does not run the complete runner or the run-tree update, and changes no file. `tools/finish_worktree.sh` uses it.

For tests, `FINISH_BRANCH_RUNNER` replaces the runner and `FINISH_BRANCH_RECORDS` the folder of evaluation records.

#### The morning guard of finishes (change `morning-guard`, idea #366)

A finish runs complete runners and pushes `main`: it must not start in the morning before the day's safety net is in place. `bash tools/morning_state.sh check` (also `--language en`) is the one reader of that state, read-only and allowed to the session. It prints one line and exits 0 (allowed), 1 (refused, every failing part named in the order watch, backup, copy) or 2 (usage); a failure of the lab folders resolution keeps its own code 78.

- **Window.** Before the morning start (`PMZILLA_RUN_MORNING_START`, 06:45 by default: the watch template's 07:10 less 25 minutes) the finish is allowed without reading anything. From the start until midnight it is allowed only when all three parts hold; there is no end of window and no fallback time, so a state that cannot be read is refused.
- **Watch.** Today's watch record (Paris day) says `termine`, which `watch.sh` writes only when no step failed. `echoue`, `interrompu`, `reporte`, `commence`, another day's record and an unreadable record refuse. A single source in error (a 404 on one feed) does not count: the collection exits 0 on individual source errors, so the record says `termine`.
- **Backup.** Today's plain dated folder `db-AAAA-MM-JJ` exists and holds exactly as many dumps as `tools/db_backup.sh` writes (4: one per schema of `SCHEMAS`, plus the mandatory partial dump of `quality`; a static test counts them in the script). A missing backups folder refuses (unlike the run-tree rule).
- **Off-site copy.** Proven by a line of the copy log dated today (`copie créée : db-<today>` or `déjà présente : db-<today>`), or by the status file of `offsite_backup.sh status` (`derniere_copie=db-<today>` for the database series); or declared postponed for today (below). A line of another day, a line without a date (written before this change), a copy of another day's backup and the absent-disk deferral line never count.
- **Refusals.** `finish_branch.sh` exits **17** and `finish_worktree.sh` exits **44**, before any change (the worktree one checks again just before the removal), with `fenêtre du matin, état du matin non vérifié`, the guard's line and `relancer quand l'état du matin est vérifié : bash tools/morning_state.sh check`. A code 17 returned to `finish_worktree.sh` by the finishing script (a race) is passed on with the recreation command. No variable, option or setting waives either refusal; `--check` never runs the guard.
- **Dated copy log.** Every line of `offsite_backup.sh copy` now starts with `AAAA-MM-JJ HH:MM:SS`, and the first says `lancement planifié` or `lancement manuel`. A manual run prints the same lines and also appends them to the copy log (`OFFSITE_COPY_LOG`, by default the output path of the copy template), without duplicating the lines of a scheduled run. A log that cannot be written gives `journal de la copie impossible à écrire` and never changes the copy's exit code.
- **Declared postponement.** When the off-site disk is knowingly out of reach for the day, run yourself `bash tools/offsite_backup.sh postpone --reason "<cause>"` (one line of 1 to 200 characters naming a cause, never a person). It appends `copie reportée déclarée : db-<today> : <cause>` to the copy log and the guard then accepts the copy part for today only; it never covers the watch or the backup part. With today's copy already proven it writes nothing. Claude Code cannot run it (deny rule): only you declare a postponement.
- **Run-tree script.** `tools/run_worktree.sh` reads the watch record and the backup folder through the same module; its own rule (final states `termine`, `echoue`, `interrompu`; no dump count; no off-site copy) is unchanged.
- **After the merge** the common body of your step files must call `bash tools/morning_state.sh check` in place of its own reading of the watch log: the dated lines no longer match the old pattern at the start of a line.

#### Light path for a specification branch (change `light-spec-finish`)

A branch that changes only regular files under `openspec/` no longer pays the complete runner (about six and a half minutes) when the tree of the up-to-date `main` is known to be green. After every successful push, `finish_branch.sh` appends the tree of the new tip of `main` to the record `pmzilla-main-validated` of the common Git folder: one line per validation, with four tab-separated columns (the tree, the kind `complet` or `allégé`, the UTC date, and the environment signature, a 64-character digest of the versions of python3, uv, git and bash and of the lab folders, computed by `bash tools/finish_env.sh --main-signature`, or `inconnue` when it cannot be computed); no name, branch, commit or path; at most the last 50 lines; the script alone writes it (never the runner, the hooks, `finish_worktree.sh` or `dbtest.sh`). A failed write prints `arbre de main non enregistré (écriture impossible)` and changes neither the result nor the exit code. A commit that lands on `main` outside a branch finish leaves a tree that the record does not hold, so the next specification branch runs the complete runner and its own finish restarts the chain.

The light path is taken only when all of these hold; otherwise the complete runner runs as before, with one line `runner complet nécessaire : <reason>` when the branch is a specification branch. Every changed path between the up-to-date `main` and the branch tip (tree to tree, a rename counted as a deletion plus an addition, merge commits included) is a regular file under `openspec/`, with no symbolic link or submodule there (`branche hors openspec/`, `lien symbolique ou sous-module sous openspec/`); the tree of `main` is in the record (`arbre de main non validé`), recorded 7 days ago at most (valid at 6 and 7 days, expired at 8, a malformed or future date counts as expired: `validation de main expirée`), with the same environment signature as the one recorded (`signature d'environnement absente de la validation` when the recorded column is not a digest, `signature d'environnement impossible : <composant> absent ou illisible` when the signature cannot be computed now, `environnement changé depuis la validation` when it differs; it stands for a change of tool version or lab folder that the tree of the files does not show); the selector can list the suites (`sélection des suites impossible`); and `FINISH_BRANCH_FORCE_RUN` is unset or empty (any non-empty value, `0` included, gives `relance forcée`). No variable can make the light path apply; check mode never takes it.

The light run replaces the complete runner by `python3 -I tools/light_validate.py <main> <tip>` (change `light-finish-touched-specs`, standard library only), which validates in strict mode only the OpenSpec items the branch touches and refuses only the strict findings the branch adds. The items are the active changes and baseline specifications whose folder differs between the up-to-date `main` and the tip and that still exist at the tip; archive folders give none (`validation OpenSpec : aucun élément à valider (archives seulement)`), and any other path under `openspec/` (configuration, project files) makes every item count. Each item is validated at the tip and in `main`'s tree exported to a temporary folder (removed on every exit); a finding is the pair level and message (the index-based path is not compared, only `ERROR` and `WARNING` count), and it is new when it is more frequent at the tip than on `main` (an item absent from `main`, such as a new change or a specification created by an archive with its provisional `TBD` purpose, has all its findings new). Green: `validation OpenSpec : <n> élément(s) touché(s), aucun nouvel avertissement strict`, plus the line `avertissements stricts déjà présents sur main, non bloquants : <items>` when some exist. Red: one line `nouvel avertissement strict : <item> : <message>` per new finding, then code 18 (`openspec validate --strict en échec, rien n'est fusionné`); a missing `openspec`, exit 126 or 127, falls back to the complete runner with the reason `openspec introuvable`), then the suites that read the OpenSpec folder, listed by `python3 tools/select_suites.py --openspec-readers` (red: code 1 with the usual phrase and `(passage allégé)`). The list is the always-selected guard suites plus the declared file `tests/openspec_readers.txt` (one suite per line, `# why` after it; `!<suite> : <reason>` for a suite that the guard flags without reading `openspec/` content; a missing file or a malformed line gives exit 2 and so the complete runner). The selector reads only that file; the guard is the test `tests/select_suites/test_openspec_readers.py`, which fails when a suite reads the real `openspec/` without being declared, so a new reader adds one line. The light path never skips on a finish record. It prints `chemin allégé : la branche ne change que openspec/ et l'arbre de main est validé`, then `passage allégé vert : <n> suite(s) au lieu du runner complet`, and the new `main` tree is recorded with the kind `allégé`. Before the merge it writes the light fingerprint `pmzilla-light-ok` (private Git folder of the worktree, the tip tree); the pre-push hook accepts it only after re-checking the same conditions on the pushed commit (changed paths, the first parent's tree in the record, expiry and environment signature), and the fingerprint is erased after a successful push and kept after code 10 so that the printed retry command works. `finish_worktree.sh` takes the same decision through the same helper (`tools/light_finish.sh`) and runs the same light pass in the worktree (code 45 when the validation is red there, code 38 with `(passage allégé)` when a suite is red), writes no finish record, and transmits code 18 with the recreation command. For tests: `FINISH_BRANCH_VALIDATE` and `FINISH_WORKTREE_VALIDATE` (a replacement is run with the same two arguments, the `main` reference and the tip), `FINISH_BRANCH_LIGHT_RUNNER`, `FINISH_WORKTREE_LIGHT_RUNNER`, `LIGHT_VALIDATE_OPENSPEC` (the OpenSpec command that the helper runs), and the clock `LIGHT_FINISH_TODAY` read by the helper alone. To force the complete runner, set `FINISH_BRANCH_FORCE_RUN=1`.

#### Local CI in a container (`tools/ci_local.sh`; change `local-ci-container`)

The portable suites run in a disposable container on the same tree that a finish will merge, so that a finish does not run them again on the Mac. Prerequisites: Colima and Docker installed (section 2).

- **Commands.** `bash tools/ci_local.sh run [<branch>]` runs `bash tests/run_all.sh --portable` in a throwaway container on the tree of the branch tip (default: a clean `HEAD`), with no network, no mount and none of your environment variables; a session may run it. `build` builds the image from `ci/Dockerfile` (network, yours only) and `up [<branch>]` (default `main`) does everything in one command: starts Colima if it does not answer, builds the image when its key changed, then runs `run`. `build` and `up` are for your own Terminal. `--help` lists the codes.
- **Codes.** 0 green; 1 red; 2 usage; 3 Docker or Colima does not answer; 4 input refused (outside the repository, unknown branch, tree not clean); 5 image missing or out of date (a key change: the Dockerfile, the pinned inventory entries, `.python-version`, the dependency line of the runner, the `# /// script` blocks of the Python scripts of `tools`, `tests` and `.claude/skills`, an npm lock) or npm layer different from the lock; 6 build failed; 7 another finish holds the lock. Codes 2 to 7 start no container. The last line is always `ci_local : verdict vert (arbre <short>, image <short key>, <n> suites, <m> suites Mac non lancées)` or `ci_local : verdict rouge (...) : <failed suites>`. A text report per run is written, owner-only, in the `ci` folder of the lab data folder (tree, image key, start time, suite lines, no path and no branch name).
- **Mac-bound list.** `tests/suites_mac.txt` lists, one suite per line with the reason, the suites that cannot run in the container (macOS-only commands, Keychain, the throwaway database, the finish scripts' own state). `bash tests/run_all.sh --portable` skips them and prints the list line; `--mac` runs only them. Neither writes nor erases a fingerprint, and `--staged` refuses both (code 2). A new macOS-only suite is added to the list with its reason; a test checks that the list matches the runner's suites.
- **Recorded verdict.** `run` removes, at start, the line of the tested tree from the file `verdicts` (in the same `ci` folder; line `<tree><TAB>vert|rouge<TAB><image key>`, no branch, path, date or person), then writes the verdict of that exact tree at the end. An interrupted run leaves no line. Only `run` (and `up`) writes the file; the finishes only read it and never start a container.
- **CI mode of the finish.** Run `bash tools/ci_local.sh run <branch>` from the branch's worktree, the branch already containing `main`, then finish with `FINISH_WORKTREE_CI=1 bash tools/finish_worktree.sh <branch>` (in the step body). After the merge check, the finish reads the line of the merged tip's tree: `verdict du conteneur vert pour l'arbre fusionné (arbre <short>, image <short key>)`, then runs the Mac pass (`bash tests/run_all.sh --mac`; red gives code 38 `passe Mac rouge dans le worktree`) instead of the complete runner. The finish record then carries `passage=ci` and the image key, and `finish_branch.sh` prints `saut du second runner : verdict du conteneur et passe Mac (arbre <short>)` instead of running its second runner; without a green line for the tip's tree it runs the runner and says why. Code **50** (finish worktree) means no usable verdict: unreadable, absent for the merged tree, or red. The message is followed by the command to run (`lancer : bash tools/ci_local.sh run <branch> (depuis le worktree), puis relancer la fin`). A merge of `main` made by the finish changes the tree, so the earlier verdict no longer counts: run `run` again on the merged branch, then finish again. Without the variable, or on the light path, nothing changes. There is no silent fallback; you may also finish without the variable (complete runner on the Mac).
- **After a key change.** Edit `ci/Dockerfile` or a pinned entry (see "Update the CI image"), then `up`: the key changes, so `up` rebuilds before running; `run` alone exits 5.
- **What stays on the Mac.** The throwaway-database pass (`tools/dbtest.sh`), the Mac-bound suites, the Mac pass of the finish, the evaluation set and every real service. For tests: `CI_LOCAL_DOCKER`, `CI_LOCAL_COLIMA`, `CI_LOCAL_VERDICTS`, `FINISH_WORKTREE_CI_VERDICTS` and `FINISH_BRANCH_CI_VERDICTS`.

### Generating the English edition (`tools/build_edition.py`)

`uv run tools/build_edition.py --language en --output <dir> [--commit <rev>] [--run-tests]` writes a tree of the committed state (default `HEAD`) for a public repository. It reads the Git objects of the commit, never the working tree, and refuses a dirty tree. `edition/manifest.yaml` classifies every tracked file (`include`, `exclude` with a reason, or `template` from `edition/templates/`); a tracked file without a rule stops the build. The tree holds no personal layer and no history. The generator never publishes: it creates no repository (no `git init` in the output), never pushes and makes no network call. `<dir>` must not exist and must lie outside the workspace.

The tree is built in `<dir>.incomplete`, then checked: the identifier scanner in tree mode (`tools/identifiants_personnels.py --arbre`) and the sensitive-name rules of the pre-commit hook, on every file. It becomes `<dir>` only when the checks pass; on a finding it is renamed `<dir>.not-publishable` (the report gives paths and line numbers, never the values). `--run-tests` copies the tree into a throwaway repository in a temporary folder and runs `bash tests/run_all.sh` there. Exit codes: 0 publishable tree; 1 tests red; 2 usage; 3 source refused; 4 output refused; 5 manifest invalid; 6 unclassified tracked paths; 7 identifier, sensitive name, library file name, book text, product version or local file in a published file listing found (the BMAD files listing is left out of the edition; an entry of a listing that names no file of the tree is reported as `local file in <listing>, line <n>`, without the listed path); 8 publication check could not run (sensitive-names list absent or empty, mapping table unreadable, library text unavailable, product-version list absent, empty or invalid, file listing unreadable). Tests: `tests/build_edition`.

**Before each generation (edition job R-33), refresh the pinned versions on purpose.** The lock files of `tools/` (section "Script dependencies" below) are shipped with the edition by the manifest (the locks of the private scripts stay out). In your Terminal, on a work branch:

```bash
cd ~/PMZilla-Lab/workspace
for f in tools/*.py; do [ -e "$f.lock" ] && uv lock --script --upgrade "$f"; done
bash tests/run_all.sh
```

Commit the locks that changed only when the run is green, then generate the edition from that commit.

The publication check has three more parts, applied to every build. **Library file names**: a line of the tree is a finding when it holds a file name of the private mapping table `tools/mapping_lab.py` (keys of `MAPPING`, `SOURCES` and `EXCLUSIONS`, values of `SOURCES`; the table is read as text, never run; case and accents ignored; names under 8 characters are too generic to count); the edition ships neutral stand-ins for that table (and for `tools/sorting_exclusions.py`), so a hit means a real file name reached another file. The default table is `tools/mapping_lab.py` of the built commit, read from its Git objects, never the working copy: code 8 when it is missing from that commit (an older commit without the table is refused), is not a regular file there, is not readable as Python or holds no name; `PMZILLA_LAB_EDITION_MAPPING` names a file on disk instead. **Book text**: a line of the tree is a finding when a sequence of consecutive words (12 by default) starting on it also occurs in the converted text of a book or of a third party's watch article (the `own-articles` folder, your own articles, and the files the watch produces, named `_*.md`, are not compared; a sequence of only numbers and punctuation never counts); the generator reads that text in memory, from the folders where the indexer reads it, and never copies, writes or prints it (code 8 when the text is unavailable). Only the watch folder may be absent (one information line, and the check goes on); an absent library folder, or library folders without a compared text, give code 8 even when the watch folder holds text. **Product version**: each line of each text file is matched against the regular expressions of `Flux-Lab/versions-produit.txt` (one per line, `#` comment lines ignored), a local file outside Git that the session never reads (code 8 when it is absent, empty or holds an invalid pattern). A finding gives a path and a line number, never the shared words, the book or the matched text.

### Publishing the English edition (`tools/publish_edition.py`)

The tool prepares the permanent local copy of the public repository, `~/PMZilla-Lab/edition-en` (a Git work tree on `main` whose `origin` is the public repository, used for publishing only). You run it yourself in the Terminal, from the workspace; the session never launches it:

```bash
uv run tools/publish_edition.py --copy ~/PMZilla-Lab/edition-en [--commit <rev>]
```

What it does, in order: it checks the copy (code 9 with `public copy refused:` and the cause: missing, inside the workspace, not a Git repository, `origin` is not the public repository, not on `main`, working tree not clean, ignored files present, `main` differs from `origin/main`); it builds the tree with the generator, test run always on, in `<copy>.build` (the generator's codes 1 to 8 are relayed, its lines unchanged); it replaces everything in the copy except its Git folder, verifies the copy against the tree and, on a failure, restores the copy to its published state (code 10, or code 11 with the two commands to run when the restore itself fails); it stages every change and prints the summary of the differences with the published version (counts, paths, a warning when the edition CHANGELOG did not change, never any file content). It never commits, pushes, fetches, tags or creates a release, and there is no option to skip the tests. It reads local Git state only: run `git -C ~/PMZilla-Lab/edition-en fetch` first if the public repository may have moved.

Then you run, in the Terminal, the commands the tool printed:

```bash
git -C ~/PMZilla-Lab/edition-en diff --cached
git -C ~/PMZilla-Lab/edition-en commit -m "Update of <D Month>"
git -C ~/PMZilla-Lab/edition-en push
```

No hook runs in the copy: the edition's hooks expect the private layer, and the checks of the tool replace them. The release lines come last, from the `current_release` of `tools/roadmap_settings.yaml` (`no current release; release lines skipped` when it is empty): `uv run --with pyyaml python tools/roadmap.py release-notes --milestone <M> --public` prints the draft of the public notes in French, limited to the closed features carrying the label `edition:public`; you have it translated into English in a Claude Code session, review it, save it as `~/PMZilla-Lab/edition-en.release-notes.md` (outside both repositories), then run `gh release create <tag> --repo <owner>/<name> --target main --title <M> --notes-file ~/PMZilla-Lab/edition-en.release-notes.md`. The tag is the lower-case milestone name, with the first free suffix from `-2` among the copy's local tags. The label is set at the end of a job: `/close` and `/start` check the changed paths against `edition/manifest.yaml` and propose it, and `uv run tools/gh_issue.py close-feature R-<n> --edition-public` sets it (on a closed feature too); `ensure-labels` creates it once.

### Working in a worktree (`tools/worktree.sh`)

A batch (`/batch <milestone>`, see "The commands") creates one worktree per kept feature with this script; you open one session per worktree with the command `/batch` printed, and `/start` resumes the feature there. Finish a batch branch with `bash tools/finish_worktree.sh <branch>` from the workspace, see below.

A Git worktree is a second folder on another branch (for example for a parallel job). It has no `Flux-Lab/`, so the sensitive-names guards (the write tool's guard and the `pre-commit` hook) refuse, closed, until they are told where the list is.

- **Create it.** `bash tools/worktree.sh <branch> [<folder>]`, from the workspace or from a worktree. The default folder is `wt-<branch with / replaced by ->` in the worktrees folder (`worktrees/` of the lab folder, or `PMZILLA_LAB_WORKTREES` when set; a relative value is made absolute from the current folder). When that folder is missing, the script creates it after its checks and says so (`dossier des worktrees créé`); if it cannot, it exits 5 and nothing is created. A folder given as second argument is used as it is, and the worktrees folder is then neither used nor created. A new branch starts from `main`. The script only checks that the list exists in the workspace; it never reads, copies or links it.
- **Variables.** In the new folder, the script writes `.claude/settings.local.json` (ignored by Git, checked first) with `PMZILLA_LAB_SENSITIVE_NAMES` (the tools) and `PMZILLA_NOMS` (the hook), so any Claude Code session opened in the folder has them. It also prints the two `export` lines and the command that opens a session there, for a Terminal.
- **Mothra npm dependencies (change `mothra-npm-ready`).** `node_modules` lives in each worktree, so a new worktree has none. When the new worktree holds `mothra/package-lock.json`, the script ends with an offer. In your Terminal it asks `dépendances npm de Mothra : lancer npm ci --offline dans mothra/ depuis le cache local ? [o/N]`: `o` runs `npm ci --offline` in `<worktree>/mothra`, from the local npm cache only, and prints `dépendances npm de Mothra installées depuis le cache local`; any other answer prints the command to type. Without a terminal (a launch from a Claude Code session, `/start` or `/batch`) nothing is asked or run: the line `dépendances npm de Mothra non installées : cd <worktree>/mothra && npm ci --offline` gives the command. A failure prints `npm ci --offline en échec (code <n>) : cache local incomplet ? lancer npm ci dans mothra/ avec le réseau, geste de l'auteur`; the worktree is kept, the exit code stays 0, and nothing is retried. The script never runs npm without `--offline`, nor `npm install`, `npm audit` or `npx`. The first install on a Mac fills the cache: you run `npm ci` in `mothra/` yourself, with the network; later worktrees then install offline. Without a lock (before Mothra's first commit) there is no offer. For tests, `WORKTREE_NPM` replaces the npm command and `WORKTREE_TERMINAL` (`1` or `0`) forces standard input to count as a terminal or not; `deny` rules forbid a Claude Code session to set either variable (`Bash(*WORKTREE_TERMINAL=*)`, `Bash(*WORKTREE_NPM=*)`, `Bash(export WORKTREE_*)`), so a session can neither answer the offer in your place nor replace npm. The npm suite has no skip rule: a complete run keeps running it, and missing dependencies stay a named failure.
- **Doctor check `mothra-npm`.** `uv run tools/doctor.py` reads Mothra in the workspace, without the network, never in a worktree, even when launched from one: after the offer of a new worktree, `mothra-npm` turns ok only once the workspace's `mothra/` has its own `node_modules` too. Skipped with `pas de verrou npm de Mothra` when `mothra/package-lock.json` is absent, and with the doctor's usual content-folder reason when the workspace cannot be found; a warning, one line per finding, for a Node (`node --version`) other than `mothra/.node-version` or absent (`Node <version> différent de mothra/.node-version (<version fixée>)`, compared exactly, never by prefix), a missing `mothra/.node-version` (`mothra/.node-version absent : version de Node non vérifiée`), a missing `node_modules` (`node_modules absent dans mothra/`) and a `node_modules` whose recorded lock (`node_modules/.package-lock.json`) does not match the lock (`node_modules périmé : verrou plus récent ou différent`: the `packages` entries are compared by version and integrity; an unreadable recorded lock counts as stale); ok otherwise. Never a problem: the runner's named failure is the blocking signal. It compares the locks, not the installed contents.
- **Codes.** 0 created; 2 usage; 3 the list is missing from the workspace (nothing is created); 4 the folder exists or the branch is open elsewhere; 5 Git failed, the worktrees folder cannot be created, or the local settings are not ignored (nothing is written).
- **Commit.** `git commit` alone: the variables come from the session environment, never from a prefix. Run `bash tests/run_all.sh --staged` in the worktree as in the workspace.
- **Finish and remove.** `tools/finish_branch.sh` refuses while `main` is open in another folder. Remove a worktree with `git worktree remove <folder>`, then delete the branch if it is merged.
- **Finish a batch branch (`tools/finish_worktree.sh <branch>`).** Run it in your own Terminal from the workspace, once the session of that worktree has ended; Claude Code cannot launch it (deny rules). It checks everything before removing anything, in this order: codes 20 to 29 (usage, workspace on `main`, branch and worktree found, clean worktree; throwaway ignored files such as the local settings and caches do not block); the pre-check (`tools/finish_branch.sh --check <branch>` in the workspace); the complete runner (`bash tests/run_all.sh`, never filtered) in the worktree, with its output shown as it runs, then a green line that says whether a finish record was written (see below); the same check again just before the removal. Then it removes the worktree, switches the workspace to the branch and runs `tools/finish_branch.sh` there with its input closed, which runs the complete runner a second time unless a finish record matches (the skip is on by default, see below), then returns its code. **Finish record (change `test-speed`).** **The skip is on by default** (change `skip-default-on`; the former rule "never set the setting at `1`" no longer applies once that change is merged): `finish_worktree.sh` writes the record when the setting `FINISH_WORKTREE_ALLOW_SKIP` is absent, empty or exactly `1` and every other condition holds, so a finish from a worktree then runs one complete runner. Any other value (`0`, `oui`, `11`, a space) turns the skip off: no record is written, the green line reads `saut non autorisé (réglage désactivé) : le script de fin le relancera`, the token is still passed, and `finish_branch.sh` runs its complete runner a second time (reason `saut non autorisé (réglage désactivé)`, written to the durations file like the others). Turn the skip off for one finish with `FINISH_WORKTREE_ALLOW_SKIP=0 bash tools/finish_worktree.sh <branch>`; the stability check also covers directories (change `test-db-guards`): the worktree root and every folder that holds a tracked file, directly or in a subfolder, are checked against the same marker with the same change-time test, so a tracked folder renamed during the runner and put back, or an untracked file created then removed, gives the line `worktree modifié pendant le runner` and no record (no path is printed; a failed listing counts as a change). After a green complete run in the worktree, with the setting absent, empty or at `1`, `finish_worktree.sh` writes the one-time record `pmzilla-finish-tested` in the common Git folder (owner-only, no path), but only when the complete-run fingerprint that the real runner wrote in the worktree equals the tree of the branch tip; it deletes any older record before the runner starts, and passes a random token to `finish_branch.sh` through `FINISH_BRANCH_TESTED_TOKEN` (never printed). `finish_branch.sh` reads the record only when that token is set, deletes it at once, and skips its complete runner (`runner complet déjà vert dans le worktree pour l'arbre fusionné : second lancement sauté`) only when the token, the branch, the tip, the tree, the predicted merged tree of `main` and the branch (`git merge-tree --write-tree`) and an environment signature (tool versions, lab folders, hooks folder; hash only) all match. Otherwise it prints `second lancement nécessaire : <reason>` and runs the runner as before; the reasons are, in order, `relance forcée`, `saut non autorisé (réglage désactivé)`, `fiche de fin absente`, `fiche de fin illisible`, `jeton différent`, `autre branche`, `autre sommet`, `arbre du sommet différent`, `arbre fusionné différent`, `arbre fusionné non calculable` and `environnement différent`. On a skip it writes the tested tree as the workspace complete-run fingerprint just before the merge, so the pre-push hook accepts the push without a run; if the merge commit's tree differs from the tested tree (never expected) it erases that fingerprint, says so, and the pre-push hook runs the complete runner itself. A `finish_branch.sh` launched by hand has no token: it always runs its runner and neither reads the record nor writes durations. `FINISH_BRANCH_FORCE_RUN` set to any non-empty value (`0` and `non` included) forces the complete run and keeps priority over the setting, whatever its value (setting absent included), with the reason `relance forcée`; it can only force, never skip, and the record is still read and deleted. Each skip (`fin-saut`) and each second run (`fin-relance`, with its reason, duration and verdict) is also written as a line of the durations file in the lab data folder, for a review after a week. Not covered by the signature: ignored files inside the checkout and the folder path; a suite green in the worktree and red in the workspace would reach `main` and show at the next complete run. The branch that introduced this mechanism was itself finished with two complete runs. The freeze of the tests and the runner lock are not read by the script: your stage file sets the freeze before it calls the script.
  - Refusals before any change (the worktree is still there and listed, the workspace on `main`, the finishing script not run): 32 change OpenSpec not archived, 33 validating evaluation run missing, 34 `main` not up to date, 35 the branch does not contain `main`, 36 `main` open in another folder (the codes 5, 6, 7, 8 and 12 of `--check`), 41 the green throwaway-database run is missing (code 14 of `--check`: the branch changes database code; the refusal comes before the runner, so a branch without the record is not made to wait for it), 37 pre-check impossible (any other code of the check, or a failed launch, with its code), 38 the complete runner is not green in the worktree (with its code), 40 the branch tip moved during the runner. Code 39 is reserved and never returned. The second check maps to the same 32 to 37; a file left by a suite in the worktree makes it refuse with 37.
  - **Finish lock (change `multi-session-piloting`).** Code 42, right after the usage check (as soon as the repository is found): another branch finish is running; nothing is changed, checked or waited for. The script holds the lock (a folder `pmzilla-fin-en-cours` in the common Git folder) to its exit, whatever the outcome, passes its token to the check, the runner and `tools/finish_branch.sh` (which then takes no second lock), and after an exit 0 removes the handoff note of the branch. See "Several sessions".
  - **Morning guard (change `morning-guard`).** Code 44: `bash tools/morning_state.sh check` refuses inside the morning window (see "The morning guard of finishes"). Checked right after the finish lock (42) and before refusal 22, and again just before the worktree is removed; nothing is changed (the worktree stays, the workspace stays on `main`, the finishing script is not launched). A code 17 of `finish_branch.sh` is passed on with the recreation command. No variable waives it.
  - **Process check (change `test-speed-2`, idea #285).** The finish record is written only if, just before the runner and just after it, no other process has its current folder in the worktree (or in a folder inside it): `lsof -w -n -P -d cwd -F pn`, twice, the script's own process excluded. A process found, or a check that is impossible (`lsof` absent, a non-zero exit, or a listing that does not name the script's own process), means no record and one line (`un autre processus a son dossier courant dans le worktree` or `vérification des processus impossible`, then `le script de fin le relancera`): `finish_branch.sh` runs its second complete runner, as when the setting is off. The line comes after the checks on a worktree changed during the runner and before the fingerprint and the setting. No exit code is added; the script never signals, waits for or names those processes (no number, no command). A Claude Code session, a terminal or an editor terminal left in the worktree keeps its current folder there for its whole life: that is the cause this check closes. It does not see: an action by absolute path from a current folder outside the worktree (a shell of the workspace that renames a folder of the worktree), a process that starts and ends between the two listings, processes of another user or of the system, a process that holds a folder open without having it as current folder, the container virtual machine and file-event consumers, a process whose current folder was moved out of the worktree. It is not a proof that limit 4 of the skip is closed.
  - **Light pass (change `light-spec-finish`).** Before its run in the worktree, the script takes the same light decision as `finish_branch.sh` (see "Finishing a branch"; `main` is the most advanced of local `main` and `origin/main`, divergent references give the complete runner). Light: it runs `python3 -I tools/light_validate.py <main> <tip>` (strict validation of the touched OpenSpec items, refusing only the new strict findings; see "Finishing a branch"), then the suites that read `openspec/` in the worktree (code 45 when the validation is red, nothing changed; code 38 with `(passage allégé)` when a suite is red), writes no finish record (`fiche de fin sans objet`), and the finishing script repeats the light pass. Otherwise the complete runner runs as before, with `runner complet nécessaire : <reason>` for a specification branch. Code 18 of the finishing script is transmitted with the recreation command, like codes 1 to 9.
  - Codes 30 and 31 report a failed removal or switch (31 says the worktree is removed, the workspace is on `main`, and gives `bash tools/worktree.sh <branch>` to recreate it). When the finishing script itself fails with a code that leaves the branch unmerged (1 to 9), the worktree is already removed: the script prints `git switch main && bash tools/worktree.sh <branch>` to resume. After a green worktree run, that happens only when the race of a few seconds with the remote, or a suite green in the worktree and red in the workspace, strikes: the work is intact on the branch and `main` is unchanged. A code from 0 to 13 passed through always means the worktree is removed.
  - **Finish under the lock (change `finish-under-lock`).** Everything your step file used to do before calling the finish now happens inside `finish_worktree.sh`, under its lock. Order: usage, lock (42), morning guard (44), checks 21 to 29; then a **first check before any change** (codes 0, 8 and 14 go on; 5, 6, 7, 12 and 19 refuse at once, nothing changed); then, when the branch does not contain the most advanced of local `main` and `origin/main` (one fetch; local `main` is never moved; a failed fetch, `main distante injoignable : aucune fusion`, or a local and a remote `main` that diverge, `main locale et main distante divergent : aucune fusion`, merge nothing and the check is run again, which refuses), a **preview** of the merge (a predicted conflict exits **46** with `fusion de main dans la branche impossible : conflit prévu sur <n> fichier(s), rien n'est changé` and the paths, no merge started) and the **merge** `git merge --no-edit` in the worktree, empty input, carrying the lock token (`main fusionnée dans la branche (<tip>)`, or `main déjà contenue dans la branche : aucune fusion`); a merge refused by a hook, or any failure, is aborted and exits **46** with `fusion annulée, rien n'est changé`. The check is run again after a merge; its code 14 runs `bash tools/dbtest.sh` in the worktree under the lock (`passage dbtest requis pour l'arbre du sommet`, then `passage dbtest vert` and a new check; red: **48** with its code; 14 again: 41). The light decision, the runner and the finish record are computed on the merged tip, so the finishing script skips its second runner when `main` has not moved. Code **49**: the check refuses with 19 (the branch touches another change). Code **50** is returned in CI mode (`FINISH_WORKTREE_CI=1`) when the container verdict of the merged tree is unreadable (`verdict du conteneur illisible (dossier de données introuvable)`), absent (`verdict du conteneur absent pour l'arbre fusionné`) or red (`verdict du conteneur rouge pour l'arbre fusionné`), followed by the command to run (see "Local CI in a container"); code 47 (the old external freeze file) is gone and is never returned. A merge already made stays in the branch after a later refusal, and the refusal says so on the next line: `main est fusionnée dans la branche ; rien d'autre n'est changé`. An interruption (129, 130, 143) prints `finish_worktree : interrompu (code <n>) : <state>` and aborts only a merge the script started and that is not finished (`fusion de main annulée, rien n'est changé`). After a finishing script that exits 0, the line `finish_worktree : terminé : main <before> -> <after>` is printed only when local `main` contains the finished tip; otherwise `succès non établi : main ne contient pas le sommet de la branche`.
