A finish record left by an earlier run is erased right after the lock; the record this run writes is erased on exit unless the finishing script was launched, and it is written only when the complete-run fingerprint was rewritten by this runner (an older fingerprint equal to the tip gives `empreinte complète non réécrite par ce runner`).
  - **Throwaway database under the lock.** While a finish holds the lock, `bash tools/dbtest.sh` launched by anything else exits **3** with `dbtest : fin de branche en cours (<branche>), lancement refusé` (no container started, no record touched); a stale lock gives `dbtest : verrou de fin périmé, ignoré` and the run goes on; the finish's own pass carries the token and runs.
  - **Your step files.** A finish step file no longer creates a freeze file, merges `main` by hand, or runs `dbtest.sh` before the finish: it checks the morning state, asks its yes/no question, calls `bash tools/finish_worktree.sh <branch>` (the script asks nothing) and counts a success only on the line `finish_worktree : terminé : main <before> -> <after>`. A step file that chains `bash tools/dbtest.sh` in the worktree and the finish goes back to the workspace (`cd ~/PMZilla-Lab/workspace`) between the two: a terminal left in the worktree makes the finish refuse. Commit the archive of the change before any merge simulation: a simulation made before the archive does not see the spec conflict that the archive may create.
  - **`/start` in a worktree.** Started from the workspace on `main`, `/start <id>` creates the job's worktree with `bash tools/worktree.sh`, sets the Status to In progress, prints `cd <worktree> && claude` and `/start <id>` to type in that session, and stops; every hand-over names `bash tools/finish_worktree.sh <branch>`. `/implement` refuses in the workspace on `main` and names the same worktree command.
  - **Two hooks guard `main` under the lock (D17).** `pre-merge-commit` refuses a merge commit while another finish holds the lock (`pre-merge-commit : fin de branche en cours (<branche>), fusion refusée`), and `reference-transaction` (Git 2.28 or later) refuses any update of `refs/heads/main` in the same case: a fast-forward, `reset --hard`, `pull`, `update-ref`, a rebase ending on `main` (`reference-transaction : fin de branche en cours (<branche>), mise à jour de main refusée`). Other branches move freely; with no lock folder, `reference-transaction` exits at once without starting Python; a stale lock prints `verrou de fin périmé, ignoré` and lets through; the finish itself, its merge, its `merge --abort`, its restoring steps and the move of `main` by `finish_branch.sh` carry the token and pass. No variable lifts either refusal. Limit: `git pack-refs`, or the `git gc --auto` that some commands start, files `main` away from its loose file through the same kind of update; under another finish's lock that tidying is refused, `main` keeps its value, and the command that started it is not affected (it is done at the next `gc`). **After the merge of this change, run in your Terminal, from the workspace: `bash tools/install_hooks.sh`, then `bash tools/install_hooks.sh --verify` (six hooks).** **Unblocking (you only).** If the hook blocks `main` by mistake: (1) `uv run tools/pilot.py freeze --check` says whether a finish really holds the lock; (2) if a live finish holds it, wait (`uv run tools/pilot.py freeze --wait 60`); (3) a stale or orphaned lock already lets updates through (the hooks say `verrou de fin périmé, ignoré`) and the next finishing script replaces it; neither finishing script's help gives a removal command, so the lock folder is never removed by hand, least of all from a session; (4) as a last resort, rename `.git/hooks/reference-transaction` in your own Terminal, make the update, then run `bash tools/install_hooks.sh` again; (5) note the case in an issue through `tools/gh_issue.py`.
  - For tests: `FINISH_WORKTREE_FINISH`, `FINISH_WORKTREE_CHECK`, `FINISH_WORKTREE_RUNNER` and `FINISH_WORKTREE_DBTEST` replace the four commands. It never forces, pushes or deletes a branch itself, and its only merge is the one of `main` into the branch described above. Detail of every code: the header of the script.
- The personal layer (`.pmzilla/`) is not in a worktree: the identifier scanner then works with the account, home-folder and root-path identifiers only.

### Several sessions (`tools/pilot.py`, `/pilot`; change `multi-session-piloting`)

Working with several sessions at once (one orchestrating session, one agent per worktree, the finishing scripts typed by you one at a time) rests on four pieces.

- **See what runs: `uv run tools/pilot.py status`** (or `/pilot`). Read-only; the list comes from Git at each call, never from a registry file. Header `pilot : <n> worktree(s)`, then one line per linked worktree other than the run tree: folder (relative to the lab folder), branch, link (`R-<n>` from the branch name; `change <name>` followed by the feature when the queue reader's snapshot names it; a dash otherwise; no network, no `gh`), clean or `<k> fichier(s) modifié(s)` (counts only), `+<a>/-<b> sur main`, age of the last commit, `occupé` / `libre` / `inconnu` (a process has its current folder there, none, or the listing is impossible), `fusionnée, à retirer` and `note : <text>`. Then the workspace line, the finish lock line and the run tree line. No absolute path, file name or process number is printed. Exit 0 printed, 2 usage, 3 outside a clone. `--language en` for English.
- **One finish at a time: the finish lock.** `tools/finish_worktree.sh` and `tools/finish_branch.sh` (normal run, never `--check`) take a folder `pmzilla-fin-en-cours` in the common Git folder for their whole run (files `pid`, `debut`, `branche`, `jeton`; owner only; never tracked) and release it on exit, whatever the outcome, only when the token is theirs. A second finish is refused at once, never queued: `finish_branch.sh` exit 15, `finish_worktree.sh` exit 42, with `une autre fin de branche est en cours (<branche>, depuis HH:MM)`. The holder exports its token as `FINISH_LOCK_TOKEN` to the check, the runner, the finishing script and the hooks, so none of them is refused. A lock whose process is dead, older than `PMZILLA_FINISH_LOCK_MAX_AGE` seconds (21600 by default) or without a readable `pid` for more than ten seconds is stale: the next finishing script reports `verrou de fin périmé, repris` and replaces it; the runner, the hook and the pilot tool ignore it.
- **The test freeze, enforced.** While another process holds the lock, `bash tests/run_all.sh` (complete, targeted and filtered) exits 4 with `run_all : fin de branche en cours (<branche>), lancement refusé`, and the `pre-commit` hook refuses a commit with `pre-commit : fin de branche en cours (<branche>), commit refusé` (the owner bypass variable does not lift it). `uv run tools/pilot.py freeze --check` (exit 0 free, 4 running) and `freeze --wait <minutes>` (1 to 120; exit 4 when the minutes are spent) replace the external freeze file and the absolute-path check of the agents' prompts. Nothing here creates, removes or changes the lock except the two finishing scripts.
- **Handoff notes.** `uv run tools/pilot.py note <branch> "<text>"` sets one line (at most 200 characters, one line) for the worktree of a branch, `note <branch> --clear` removes it; `status` shows it. The text passes the write tool's guard first (sensitive names, personal identifiers, ticket keys): exit 5 on a finding, 6 when the guard cannot run (list absent or empty), 7 when no worktree holds the branch, 8 when the note cannot be written. Notes live in the `pmzilla-notes` folder of the common Git folder, owner only, never tracked; `finish_worktree.sh` removes the note of its branch after an exit 0.
- **Agent rules.** `docs/pmzilla/agent-preamble.md` holds the standing rules to paste at the top of every agent prompt (Edit or Write only, never a script, `sed -i` or a redirection; no command piped into `python3` or `grep`; only the allowed commands; `uv run tools/pilot.py freeze --wait 60` before each test run and commit; stop and report on a permission refusal; no protected reads; no names) and the commit rule per agent kind: an implementation agent commits on its own branch after a green run and the freeze command; a framing agent (`spec-writer`) never commits, the orchestrating session commits its work after review; review and search agents never commit.
- **Your steps.** The deny rules that keep a session from creating, removing or moving the lock (`rm`, `rmdir`, `mv`, `mkdir` and `touch` on a path holding `pmzilla-fin-en-cours`) go in `.claude/settings.json` by you; after the merge, retire the external freeze file from your step-file template and replace the agents' standing block with the preamble. Variables for tests: `PILOT_NOW`, `PILOT_LSOF`, `PILOT_POLL_SECONDS`, `PILOT_WAIT_UNIT_SECONDS`.

### Run worktree (`tools/run_worktree.sh`)

The scheduled jobs (watch and catch-up, database backup, off-site copy and restore test, doctor) and the library server run the code of a separate folder, the run tree `~/PMZilla-Lab/run`. It is a linked Git worktree of the repository, with a detached head equal to the tip of local `main`, never on a branch. The development checkout `~/PMZilla-Lab/workspace` is therefore free to be on any branch at any time: the old rule "the workspace on `main` before 7:10" no longer exists. The content (watch, library, flow, journal, context file, memory) stays in the development checkout; the scheduled scripts export it as the content root `PMZILLA_LAB_WORKSPACE`, and run code from the run tree (the code root). A scheduled watch run prints both lines `Dossier de code : …` and `Dossier de contenu : …` after its header. If the content root is the run tree, the watch stops (`=== Veille arrêtée : le dossier de contenu est le dossier d'exécution ===`, one notification, the day recorded as failed, exit 1).

Commands, in your Terminal (Claude Code cannot run `create` or `update`; `status` and `--help` are allowed). Add `--language en` for English phrases:

- `bash tools/run_worktree.sh create`: adds the run tree, detached at the tip of `main`, then prepares the dependencies. Already there: says so, exit 0.
- `bash tools/run_worktree.sh update`: fast-forward to the tip of `main` (never a reset, a clean or a fetch), then prepares the dependencies again, also when the tree was already at the tip, so a rerun recovers a failed preparation. `tools/finish_branch.sh` runs it after each merge.
- `bash tools/run_worktree.sh status`: read-only report, nothing fetched or prepared.
- **Dependency preparation.** From the run tree, `uv sync --locked --script` for each scheduled script with a dependency header and a lock file (`composants.py`, `dashboard.py`, `doctor.py` and `db.py` included), so that the first scheduled run after an update needs no network. It writes no lock file and no tracked file.

| Code | Meaning |
|---|---|
| 0 | created, present, updated, up to date, dependencies prepared (`status`: clean at the tip of `main`) |
| 1 | a Git command failed |
| 2 | usage |
| 3 | development checkout or local `main` not found |
| 4 | run tree missing, or the folder exists and is not the run tree (`create`) |
| 5 | local changes in the run tree |
| 6 | the run tree is on a branch, or off the history of `main` |
| 7 | update postponed: a scheduled run is in progress, or the morning runs of the day are not finished (see "The morning state") |
| 8 | `status` only: behind `main` |
| 9 | dependencies not prepared (`create`, `update`) |

`finish_branch.sh` exits 13 when the merge is done and pushed but the run tree could not be updated (any of the codes above, or a failed preparation); run `bash tools/run_worktree.sh update` later.

**Never update during a run.** The update reads, and never takes, the watch lock `veille.verrou` and three markers in the lab data folder: `sauvegarde.encours` (backup), `horsmac-copie.encours` and `horsmac-restauration.encours` (off-site copy and restore test). Each is a folder created by `mkdir` holding `pid` and `debut` (start instant in seconds). It is held while its process is alive and its age is within the maximum (lock: `PMZILLA_WATCH_LOCK_MAX_AGE`; markers: `PMZILLA_RUN_MARKER_MAX_AGE`; both 21600 seconds by default). A dead process or a greater age makes it stale: the update reports it and goes on. A missing or unreadable `pid` counts as held for the first 10 seconds. The backup and off-site scripts create and remove their own marker and log `Repère de passage périmé, repris`, `Repère de passage déjà tenu : passage sans repère propre` or `Repère de passage impossible à écrire` (a marker never stops the job). It refuses and says so, never waits; run it again later. A catch-up launch starting during the few seconds of an update remains a residual risk.

**The morning state (replaces the two fixed windows).** From the morning start (`PMZILLA_RUN_MORNING_START`, `HH:MM` in the system time zone, 06:45 by default) to the end of the morning period (`PMZILLA_RUN_MORNING_END`, 22:00 by default, the latest time of `watch.sh`), the update is postponed (code 7) until the morning runs of the day are finished. Before the start and after the end, the state is not read. A lock or a marker held is checked first, at any hour.

- **Watch part.** Finished when the record of the day's watch run holds today's date (Paris day) and a final state: `termine`, `echoue` or `interrompu`. Not finished with another date, or today's date with `commence` or `reporte`. The record is only read, never written.
- **Backup part.** Finished when the backups folder holds the plain dated folder of today (`db-AAAA-MM-JJ`, system date). A labelled folder does not count. Only the existence of that one folder is tested: the backups folder is never listed or read. The backups folder comes from `tools/lab_paths.sh` (`DB_BACKUP_SAUVEGARDES` when set, else `sauvegardes` under the lab data folder). When it does not exist, the backup part does not count.
- **Fallback time.** When the state cannot be read (record absent or unreadable, backups folder unreadable), the update is postponed until the fallback time (`PMZILLA_RUN_FALLBACK_TIME`, 09:30 by default), then goes on with an information line. A readable part that is not finished still postpones.
- **Key phrases** (English with `--language en`): `run_worktree: update postponed, morning runs not finished (watch)` or `(backup)` (the first part not finished is named, watch first); `run_worktree: update postponed, morning runs state unreadable, before 09:30`; `run_worktree: morning runs state unreadable, fallback time passed`. When the watch is what waits, two lines follow: `run_worktree: waiting for the record of the day's watch run, state: reported` (or `started`, `of another day`) and `Once the watch is unblocked: bash tools/run_worktree.sh update`; they reach the cause lines of the exit 13 of the finishing script. No file name and no path is printed.
- **Removed.** The windows `PMZILLA_RUN_MORNING_WINDOW` and `PMZILLA_RUN_BACKUP_WINDOW` and the phrase `update postponed, scheduled runs window` no longer exist: the old variables have no effect.

**Rules.** Never edit a file, commit or open a Claude Code session in the run tree: deny rules refuse edits there and the `pre-commit` hook refuses a commit (`pre-commit : commit refusé dans le dossier d'exécution`). Reading it is allowed. The running library server keeps the old code until the next session.

**Switch-over (once, by you, after the morning jobs, outside the windows).** Preconditions: no parity marker left in the lab data folder, R-44 merged with its catch-up job installed, this change merged and the development checkout on `main`.

1. `bash tools/run_worktree.sh create`, then `bash tools/run_worktree.sh status` (code 0).
2. `bash tools/install_links.sh`, then `bash tools/install_links.sh --verify`: every link resolves into the run tree.
3. Re-render and reload the backup and doctor jobs (the launchd step of the install script), and the two off-site jobs (their commands, section 14.7): they now launch their links. The watch and catch-up jobs already launch the `watch.sh` link: no reload.
4. `zsh ~/PMZilla-Lab/watch.sh` in the Terminal. Expected in the Terminal output (a manual run does not write to the watch log), in this order: the header `=== YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM ===`, `Dossier de code : ~/PMZilla-Lab/run`, `Dossier de contenu : ~/PMZilla-Lab/workspace`, the usual steps, `=== Veille terminée sans erreur ===`. Articles are written in the development checkout, nothing in the run tree's content folders. `=== Veille non lancée : un passage est déjà en cours ===` means a run holds the lock: wait and launch again. The refusal above means roll back.
5. Open a new Claude Code session: the library server answers from the run tree. The next morning, read the watch log, the backup log and the dashboard; the doctor reports the run tree ok.

**Rollback**: `bash tools/install_links.sh --target workspace`; the templates launch links, so no reload. **Removal**: after a rollback, `git worktree remove` on the run tree folder, from the development checkout; never `git worktree prune`, which could drop the records of other worktrees.

### How the lab folders are found (`tools/lab_paths.py`, `tools/lab_paths.sh`)

No tool of `tools/` holds a literal path to the lab folder. Two twin modules, one in Python and one in shell (sourced by bash and zsh), resolve the folders the same way:

- **Code root**: the work tree that holds the running file, links followed (the run tree when a script is launched through its link of the lab folder).
- **Content root** (library, watch, `Flux-Lab/`): `PMZILLA_LAB_WORKSPACE` when it is set; else the main checkout of the repository, found through Git from the code root (so a linked worktree finds the development checkout). It is never the code root, a folder under your home folder or the current folder.
- **Lab folder**: the parent of the content root. **Run tree**: `PMZILLA_LAB_RUN`, else the `run` folder of the lab folder. **Data folder**: `PMZILLA_LAB_DATA`, else `~/.pmzilla-lab`. **Context folder** (organisation context, outside every repository): `PMZILLA_LAB_CONTEXT`, else the `contexte` folder of the lab folder (`~/PMZilla-Lab/contexte`). The modules never create, list or open anything in it.
- **Worktrees folder** (always the last line printed): `PMZILLA_LAB_WORKTREES`, else the `worktrees` folder of the lab folder. The shell function `lab_paths_worktrees_folder <content root>` gives it for a given content root without running Git. The modules never create, list or open anything in it; `tools/worktree.sh` creates it when it is missing.
- **Local files ignored by Git** (`Flux-Lab/noms-sensibles.txt`, `Flux-Lab/versions-produit.txt`, `.pmzilla/remplacements-bmad.tsv`, `.pmzilla/identifiants.txt`, `.pmzilla/contexte.md`): read in place, at the path of their variable when set, else in the content root. Nothing copies or links them into a worktree.

When the content root cannot be found, the tool stops before any step with exit code 78 (configuration error) and one of two key phrases, which never print a path: `dossier de contenu introuvable` (no variable and no Git clone: set `PMZILLA_LAB_WORKSPACE` or run from a clone of the repository) or `PMZILLA_LAB_WORKSPACE désigne un dossier absent` (the variable names a folder that does not exist; English tools print `content folder not found` and `names a missing folder`). The morning summary, the session-start reminders and the doctor do not stop: the parts that need the content root are reported as unknown. To see the answers, run `python3 tools/lab_paths.py` (eight lines `name=path`: `code_root`, `content_root`, `lab_folder`, `run_tree`, `data_folder`, `backups_folder`, `context_folder`, `worktrees_folder`).

### Lab folder layout (change `lab-folder-layout`)

The lab folder (`~/PMZilla-Lab`) is the parent of the workspace. Target layout, one line per first-level entry:

- `workspace/`: the main checkout (the development copy).
- `run/`: the run tree, a worktree used by the scheduled jobs; it stays at the root.
- `worktrees/`: one linked worktree per open branch, `wt-<branch with / replaced by ->` (resolved by `lab_paths`, see "How the lab folders are found").
- `etapes/`: the step files; the loose files of the root move here.
- `archives/`: the archived former team table, the trash folder and the public-edition copy, under their current names.
- `explorations/`: the exploration folder of a BMAD version.
- `contexte/`: the organisation context folder (see "Organisation context").
- the links that the scheduled jobs, the MCP configuration and the lab-folder script links target stay at the root.

Mothra is the `mothra/` folder of the workspace (ADR 0002, Q1), with no entry at the root of the lab folder and no lab-paths key; its worktrees are ordinary worktrees in `worktrees/`; `node_modules` and `dist` live in each worktree and are ignored by Git; secrets stay in the Keychain, no real data, no backup of its own (ADR 0002, invariant 13); a separate repository belongs to the hosting slice.

**Moving the existing worktrees (a step you run yourself, once).** Close every Claude Code session of the lab, make sure that no branch finish is running and that the deny rules for `archives/` are in `.claude/settings.json`.

1. For each worktree of `git -C <workspace> worktree list` that sits at the root of the lab folder and is named `wt-*`: `git -C <workspace> worktree move <lab folder>/wt-<name> <lab folder>/worktrees/wt-<name>`. Git updates both links itself. Git refuses a locked worktree: leave it in place. The run tree and the root links are not moved.
2. Only if a worktree folder was moved by hand (Finder or `mv`): `git -C <workspace> worktree repair <new folder>`. A worktree shown as missing is repaired, never dropped from the list.
3. Check: `git -C <workspace> worktree list` shows each moved worktree under `worktrees/`, none shown as prunable, and `git -C <new folder> status` works.
4. Move the regular files of the root (not folders, not links) into `etapes/`. Create `archives/` and `explorations/`, then move the former team table, the trash folder and the edition copy into `archives/`, and the BMAD exploration folder into `explorations/`.
5. Reopen the sessions from the new worktree folders. Claude Code treats a moved folder as a new project: what it keeps per folder is not migrated.

### Mothra sessions: npm, npx and node (change `mothra-session-guards`)

A Claude Code session never installs, updates or publishes a package: you alone run `npm install`, and the `npm audit` that conditions a commit of the lock file (ADR 0002); a session may run `npm audit` without `fix` as a read-only report (author decision of 2026-10-21). Deny rules, declared in `tests/settings_deny/test_settings_deny_npm.py` and posed by you in `.claude/settings.json` through a step file, refuse to sessions:

- `npm install`, `npm i`, `npm add`, `npm ci` (`npm ci --offline` included), `npm update`, `npm audit fix` and `npm publish`;
- `npx`, `npm exec` and its alias `x`;
- `node -e`, `--eval`, `-p`, `--print` (combined flags such as `-pe` included) and `node -` (a program on standard input);
- `pnpm` (with `pnpx`, `pn` and `pnx`), `yarn`, `bun`, `bunx` and `corepack`, as whole commands.

This is the reduced block decided on 2026-10-20 (the exact forms of #383 and of the spec, 273 rules; `python3 tests/settings_deny/test_settings_deny_npm.py --familles` prints it by family). Condition: no first code commit under `mothra/` (Mothra's slice 0) without the PreToolUse hook that resolves npm, npx and node commands as npm itself does; that hook then retires the patterns it makes useless.

Each rule covers the bare form, options before the subcommand (`npm --prefix mothra install`, `npm -w <package> ci`), `cd <folder> &&` and `cd <folder>;`, `env` with or without variables, and the absolute paths of your installation: fnm's default alias (`~/.local/share/fnm/aliases/default/bin/`, also written `$HOME/…` and `/Users/<you>/…`) and the Homebrew prefix (`/opt/homebrew/bin/`).

Sessions keep `npm test`, `npm run <script>`, `npm start`, `npm ls`, `npm view`, `npm outdated`, `npm audit` without `fix`, `npm help`, `npm --version`, `npm config get`, `node --version` and `node <file>`. `npm start` is allowed because the server guards itself: in a Claude Code session without the throwaway database it exits 4 with `SESSION_WITHOUT_THROWAWAY_DB` (mothra-slices F2). `npm ci` is refused to sessions, while the worktree offer stays allowed: the `npm ci --offline` that `tools/worktree.sh` runs after your yes on a terminal is a command run inside a script, which a deny rule never matches.

Stated effect of the rules (not hidden), as a general rule: the rules accept anything between `npm` and the refused word, so any argument word that starts with a refused word of four letters or more (`install`, `update`, `publish`, `exec`), or equals a short refused word (`i`, `add`, `ci`, `x`), triggers the refusal wherever it stands. Examples: `npm run install-check` (with or without options before `run`), so Mothra's scripts are never named after a refused subcommand; `npm help install` and `npm install --help`; `npm run build -- --target x`. On the node side, an argument of a launched file that looks like an evaluation flag (`node app.js -p 3000`) is refused too.

Accepted limits, until the hook: npm's unique abbreviations and camelCase forms (`npm upd`, `npm cleanInstall`); npm's other aliases and subcommands (`npm up`, `npm uninstall`, `npm version`, `npm config set`, `npm unpublish`…); node alone, `-i`, `--interactive`, `node < file`, `--import data:`, and a program piped to node (`… | node`: the rules are tested on the whole command line, without assuming any split on `|`); fnm's per-shell paths (`~/.local/state/fnm_multishells/<n>/bin/`, what `command -v npm` prints in a session), which change with each shell; `fnm exec`; any form launched by a script or an intermediate launcher (`command npm`, `sh -c "npm …"`, `bash -c "…"`, `time npm …`, `nohup npm …`, the same gap as the push rules); a Node script that a session writes and launches by its path (the root `CLAUDE.md` rule, the agents' ban on writing then launching a script and the synthetic loader's refusal without the throwaway database cover it). For all of these the ban holds in words, in the root `CLAUDE.md` and in `mothra/CLAUDE.md`.

**Posing the rules (your step).** After the branch's tests are committed: `python3 tests/settings_deny/test_settings_deny_npm.py --json` prints the block; your step file shows the diff, backs up `.claude/settings.json`, adds the block to `permissions.deny` after your yes, then runs `bash tests/run_all.sh settings_deny`: the real-settings tests, skipped before the pose with « règles npm et Node non posées », then pass; a partial pose fails with « garde npm : règle absente de .claude/settings.json : <règle> ».

**After an npm update.** The alias list comes from the installed npm's own table, `lib/utils/cmd-list.js` under `~/.local/share/fnm/aliases/default/lib/node_modules/npm/` (read it; once the rules are posed a session cannot run `npm <subcommand> --help`). When the components watch brings a new npm, check that table against `MOTS_NPM` in the settings-deny module (the reduced block names only the forms of #383); the full resolution belongs to the PreToolUse hook.

### Organisation context (`tools/context_lab.py`, `/context`; change `organisation-context`)

The organisation context is a set of short, dated notes about your organisation (roles, forums, flows, dependencies), kept in the context folder, outside every repository, and found by the postures through the library search. The postures also wake up with the context map, one note of at most 80 lines.

- **Folders.** The context folder is `~/PMZilla-Lab/contexte` (see "How the lab folders are found"). It holds two sub-folders reserved to you (mode 700, files 600): `originaux/`, the documents as you received them, which only you open (no tool and no session lists, opens or tests a path in it), and `notes/`, the distilled notes, one Markdown file per note, flat. Create them once with `uv run tools/context_lab.py init` (it asks for a confirmation). The tool refuses a context folder inside a Git work tree or the workspace (exit code 2).
- **Header of a note.** `title`, `date`, `nature` (`declarative`: what someone said or wrote; `measured`: figures read in a flow report or an aggregated view), `source` and `review` (later than `date`); `kind: map` for the context map only (at most one, `carte-contexte.md`). A file is named `YYYY-MM-DD-<slug>.md`. An invented template is in `docs/pmzilla/context-note-template.md`. Write roles and generic designations, never a name, and no figure of a cell under five.
- **Commands.** `/context` lists the notes; `/context add` drafts a note from your text and writes it only after your explicit yes. By hand: `uv run tools/context_lab.py list` and `check` (header and confidentiality of every note, `stale` for a note past its review date, never a body line), `add` (a draft on standard input), `review <file> <YYYY-MM-DD>` (changes only the review date). A note with a personal identifier or a name of the sensitive-names list is refused (exit code 5, line number only).
- **Indexing.** The daily run passes `--context <context folder>` to the indexer when the folder has a `notes/` sub-folder; otherwise the log says `dossier de contexte absent : notes de contexte inchangées`. The notes go into the private collection `contexte` only, embedded on this Mac only: if the embedding address (`PMZILLA_LAB_OLLAMA`) is not loopback, the run stops before any embedding call. A search that does not name the collection never returns a note; a search naming it returns the note with its date, nature, source, review date and a `stale` mark. By hand, after a first `init`: `uv run ~/PMZilla-Lab/pmzilla_lab_library.py index Bibliotheque-Lab Watch-Lab --context ~/PMZilla-Lab/contexte`.
- **What never leaves the Mac.** The notes, their titles and their headers are never sent anywhere (the edition check compares the published tree with the notes and stops with exit code 7 and `context text in <path>` on a match; the sessions read notes only through the library).
- **Move from the former folder.** The context folder used to be `~/PMZilla-Lab/Contexte-Lab`. Move it once, with every Claude Code session of the lab closed: check that `~/PMZilla-Lab/contexte` does not exist; in `.claude/settings.json`, remove the allow rule and the deny rules that name the former folder and add the deny rules of change `organisation-context` (design D10; `python3 tests/settings_deny/etat_contexte.py --json` prints the list to add); `mv ~/PMZilla-Lab/Contexte-Lab ~/PMZilla-Lab/contexte`; check the modes with `ls -ld` on the folder and its sub-folders (owner only); reopen the session. Leave no symbolic link under the former name: it would reach the originals by a path that no rule names. While the former folder is still there, the context tool refuses to run (`former context folder still present: finish the move`).

### Model and method

- **Model rule (decided on 2026-10-10).** Frame and specify on Opus, then type `/model sonnet` in the same session to execute while the context is useful. The `/implement`, `/close`, `/add-books` and `/next` headers set Sonnet; `/audit complet` stays on Opus.
- **OpenSpec rule, by kind of work.** OpenSpec for behaviour, postures, `CLAUDE.md` and publication (proposal, design, tasks, delta specs under `openspec/changes/`); a direct prompt for maintenance (a test gap, a cleanup, a fix with no behaviour change). The queue (issues of the private repository, see "The work queue on GitHub" below) records the method of each job.

### The commands

| Command | What it does | What it never does |
|---|---|---|
| `/implement <change>` | Reads the change, creates a branch ending with its name when started on `main`, implements `tasks.md` in order (tests first), commits after a green `bash tests/run_all.sh --staged`, ticks a task only when its suite passes, ends with one message. | Push, merge, run the finishing script, bypass a hook, launch the real Jira tools, the imports, the database scripts or the evaluation set (cost announced first). |
| `/close <change>` | Ticks the covered tasks, updates the status document the repository keeps (if any), archives (CHANGELOG.md is frozen: no entry) the change (every unticked task has a reason, you confirm), closes the queue feature on GitHub (Status `Done`), commits, prints `bash tools/finish_branch.sh`. | Run the finishing script, push, merge, invent a queue feature. |
| `/add-books <files>` | From file names: proposes the domains (you validate), writes the `MAPPING` line, the `EXCLUSIONS` line of a PDF duplicate (EPUB first) and the catalogue entry (`a_verifier` when deduced from the file name), applies the pending items of section 4 of `bibliotheque-lacunes.md`, prints the commands in order (conversion dry run, conversion, sort, indexing, import dry run, import, verification). The daily watch run imports into the database after indexing: a catalogue change reaches the database at the next daily run. | Run any of those commands, open a book, decide the domains. |
| `/next <duration> [mothra\|pmzilla]` | Proposes the features of the GitHub queue whose Status is `Ready`, with no open blocker and whose `not_before` date is reached, that fit the time given (all products, or only `mothra` or `pmzilla` when named); the jobs without an estimate are listed apart. | Edit the queue, start a job, invent an estimate. |
| `/batch <milestone>` | Reads the milestone through `tools/roadmap.py batch`, keeps the independent `Ready` features (sets aside those sharing files with a retained one or with an `In progress` feature), asks one yes in which you can remove features, creates one worktree per kept feature (`tools/worktree.sh`), sets each `In progress` (each write approved at the permission prompt), prints per feature its folder, branch, launch model, the `cd <folder> && claude --model <alias> "<prompt>"` line, the prompt alone and `bash tools/finish_worktree.sh <branch>` for you. | Start a session or `claude`, finish or merge a branch, push, reorder the queue, edit the current release setting. |
| `/pilot` | Runs `uv run tools/pilot.py status` (and, when you ask whether tests may run, `freeze --check`), presents the parallel sessions (worktree, branch, feature, state, finish lock, handoff note), flags what needs you and gives the finishing command of a worktree whose note says it is ready, labelled as yours. | Run a finishing script, write a note without your yes, commit, merge, push, remove a worktree, write to GitHub, read a note's file. |
| `/start <id>` | Checks the job (queue valid, Status `Ready`, no open blocker, `not_before` reached; asks you to confirm its conditions and to switch to its model), creates its branch from `main`, does it from its description (direct prompt) or through `/implement` (OpenSpec; a missing change is written by `spec-writer` and reviewed by you first), sets its Status to `In progress`, closes it when committed and green, prints `bash tools/finish_branch.sh`. Opened on an `In progress` feature on the branch `/batch` named for it, it resumes: no branch, no Status write, and it prints `bash tools/finish_worktree.sh <branch>`. | Push, merge, run the finishing script, reorder the queue, change a job's estimate, model or dependencies, start a job that is not ready. |

### The work queue on GitHub

The queue lives in the issues of the private repository (the remote `origin`), not in a document; `docs/pmzilla/application.md` keeps the rationale, and `docs/pmzilla/architecture.md` describes how the parts fit together. The old document is decommissioned.

- **Model.** A feature is one job, titled `R-<n> · <title>`, labelled `type:feature` and `theme:<theme>`. Its body starts with the marker `<!-- roadmap-id: R-<n> -->` (the idempotence key) and a fenced `roadmap` block (id, theme, estimate, method, model, rank, conditions, optional dates). An OpenSpec job has one sub-issue per numbered group of its `tasks.md` (`type:task`, `model:<opus|sonnet|haiku>`, `parallel` when tagged) and one `owner:user` sub-issue per user step. The groups of `tasks.md` carry tags at the end of the heading: `[model: opus|sonnet|haiku]` or `[user]`, plus `[parallel]`; an untagged numbered group is refused.
- **Products (rule of 2026-10-17).** The queue is shared by PMZilla and Mothra (the local web application). Every feature, task or idea of Mothra carries the label `product:mothra`; the absence of a `product:` label means PMZilla, and the existing issues are not relabelled. The title of a Mothra item starts with `Mothra : ` (after the key: `R-<n> · Mothra : <title>`). The `R-<n>` numbering stays common to both products. The branches are `feat/mothra-<name>`, and the commits of a Mothra change use the scope `mothra`. The list of products is closed (today only `mothra`); an issue carries zero or one `product:` label, and the reader's `check` refuses an unknown product or two labels. `gh_issue.py` sets the label with `--product mothra` on `new-feature` and `new-idea`, and with `--product mothra|none` on `edit` and `edit-idea` (`none` removes it); `sync-tasks` copies the `product:` label of a feature onto the sub-issues it creates; `ensure-labels` creates `product:mothra` once.
- **Mothra slices (rule of 2026-10-18).** A Mothra feature is framed read-only on Opus by the spec-writer, in vertical slices with an end-of-slice review, and nothing is created before the author's validation: `docs/pmzilla/mothra-tranches.md` (rule, review format, standard framing prompt).
- **Status.** The Project field Status has four options: `Todo`, `Ready`, `In progress`, `Done`. "Blocked" is not a status: a `Todo` feature with an open "blocked by" relation is blocked. Done features are closed as completed.
- **Milestones.** `Tarasque` holds every done job (closed milestone); the open ones, without due date, are `Hydra`, `Namazu`, `Cerberus`, `Kraken`, `Chimera`, `Golem` and `Leviathan`.
- **`gh`.** The GitHub CLI must be installed and authenticated (`gh auth login`), with the `project` token scope: `gh auth refresh -s project`, both typed in your Terminal. The tools resolve the repository from `origin` and refuse another.
- **Write tool.** `uv run tools/gh_issue.py <subcommand>` is the only write path (`new-feature`, `edit`, `status`, `block`, `unblock`, `comment`, `sync-tasks`, `new-task`, `close-step`, `close-feature`, `new-idea`, `edit-idea`, `comment-idea`, `close-idea`, `ensure-labels`, `ensure-milestone`; `new-idea --title … --body … [--milestone M] [--value 1|2|3]` files an idea, labelled `type:idea` (and `value:N` when given), outside the Project, with a milestone only when `--milestone` names an existing one; `edit R-<n> --title T` renames a feature (T is the text without the key, the command adds `R-<n> · `; an empty title, or one that already starts with a key, is refused with code 2); `edit-idea <n|#n> [--title T] [--milestone M] [--value 1|2|3]` changes the title (not empty, idea marker kept), the milestone or replaces the `value:N` label of an existing idea, and refuses an issue without the `type:idea` label; `comment-idea <n|#n> --body-file F` comments an idea, `close-idea <n|#n> [--comment-file F]` closes it after the optional comment, and both refuse, with code 7 and before any write, an issue without the `type:idea` label; `comment` stays reserved to `R-<n>` features). It never deletes, transfers or locks an issue, never deletes a label or a milestone, never creates a release or a tag. Before every request, a guard checks every text sent against the sensitive-names list (local, outside Git), the personal-identifier scanner and a ticket-key pattern; a finding refuses the request and nothing is sent, and the guard fails closed when its list is absent or empty. The output names the field and the line, never the matched value. Writes are never queued: offline, the tool refuses. No command header pre-approves it; each write goes through the permission prompt.
- **Bounds and exit code 8.** Every `gh` call has its standard input closed, prompts disabled and a time limit (`PMZILLA_GH_TIMEOUT`, 60 s by default); rate-limit waits are capped per command (`PMZILLA_GH_MAX_WAIT`, 120 s by default). Exit code 5 still means that nothing was sent. Exit code 8, `state unknown`, means that a write may have left (no answer, or the connection was lost after confirmed writes): the tool prints the check command (`uv run --with pyyaml python tools/roadmap.py show <id>`); run it, then run the same command again if needed. The tool never retries a write whose result is unknown. A scope error (`gh auth refresh -s project`) is reported only on GitHub's own scope error. After `new-feature`, the tool waits up to 15 s for GitHub to list the new issue.
- **Current release.** `tools/roadmap_settings.yaml` holds `current_release`, the milestone of the current public edition; change it at each publication, then run `uv run --with pyyaml python tools/roadmap.py check`. `/start` (`status <id> "In progress"`) moves the feature and its sub-issues into that milestone; with an empty setting nothing moves, with a closed or unknown milestone the command exits 7 before any write. Sub-issues carry the milestone of their feature (set at creation and by the sync; `edit --milestone` moves them with the feature).
- **Closing.** `close-feature` posts one comment naming the branch. `close-step <key> --reason "<text>"` closes an agent sub-issue (the reason is mandatory, posted as a comment, and the sync does not reopen it); `/close` proposes it when every unticked task of the group carries a reason, and runs it only after your yes. `close-step` also takes the key `R-<n>/u<k>` of a standalone user task (see "User task without a change" below).
- **User task without a change.** `uv run tools/gh_issue.py new-task R-<n> --title "<title>" --body-file <file> [--milestone <milestone>]` files a user step that belongs to no `tasks.md`: an issue labelled `type:task` and `owner:user` (plus the `product:` label of the feature), keyed `R-<n>/u<k>` (`k` is the next free number for that feature; the keys `g<N>` and `t<N.M>` of `sync-tasks` are never reused), linked as a sub-issue of the feature, in the feature's milestone unless `--milestone` names another existing one (unknown: code 7). The title is the text without key (the command adds `R-<n> · `; empty or already prefixed: code 2); the body file must be a non-empty regular file. The guard checks title and body; a second run with the same title creates nothing (`already exists`). `sync-tasks` never closes or reopens it; close it with `close-step R-<n>/u<k>`.
- **Reader.** `uv run --with pyyaml python tools/roadmap.py` reads the repository of `origin` (owner and name passed explicitly), with the same bounds as the write tool; a time-out falls back to the cache. It reads GitHub: `check`, `next --budget 2h` (what `/next` runs), `show`, `status`, `board`, `reminder --session-hook`; `batch --milestone <name>` (what `/batch` runs) and `release-notes --milestone <name>`, which drafts the notes of a milestone from its closed features. `next`, `status`, `board` and `batch` take `--product mothra|pmzilla`: `mothra` keeps the features labelled `product:mothra`, `pmzilla` the features without a `product:` label, and with no option the whole queue is read as before; blockers are always computed over the whole queue, so a Mothra feature blocked by a PMZilla feature stays blocked. `/next 2h mothra` passes the filter to the reader.
- **Cache.** Each successful full read writes a snapshot into the lab data folder, outside the repository. When GitHub is unreachable, the reader prints `GitHub unreachable; read from the cache of <date>` and reads the snapshot; with none, it exits 5. The session-start reminder never calls the network and prints one line from the snapshot.
- **Releases of the public edition.** One release per milestone that delivers an edition version: the release title is the milestone name, the tag is the name in lower case (`tarasque`), and a fix of the same version adds a numbered suffix (`tarasque-2`). The edition published on 2026-10-12 is `Tarasque`. You create tags and releases yourself; no tool of the repository does.
- **Launching the terminal.** Start iTerm2 from the Dock or Spotlight, never from a Claude Code session: otherwise the `CLAUDECODE` variables are inherited and the tools you run yourself refuse to start.

### Script dependencies (decisions of 2026-10-10 and 2026-10-11)

Each script of `tools/` declares its third-party packages in its `# /// script` block, imports reached through other modules of `tools/` included, even late ones inside a function; `uv run` builds and caches one environment per script. The tests add theirs with `uv run --with …`. The headers pin no version, except the `mcp>=1.2,<2` bound, and there is no `pyproject.toml`. `tests/script_dependencies` checks the declarations of every entry script.

**Pinned versions (decision of 2026-10-11, R-36; it replaces the unpinned daily use of R-10).** Each entry script that declares dependencies has its lock file next to it, `tools/<script>.py.lock`, written by `uv lock --script tools/<script>.py` and tracked; `uv run` reads it, for daily use as for a fresh clone of the edition, which receives the same files. The tests still add their packages with `uv run --with …`; the real check of `tests/script_dependencies` (`uv run --isolated tools/<script>.py --help`, skipped without network nor cache) runs the locked versions, and the suite fails when an entry script with dependencies has no lock, when a lock no longer lists the packages of its header, or when a lock has no script. A new entry script with dependencies: run `uv lock --script tools/<script>.py` and commit the lock with it. The versions only move when you refresh them on purpose ("Generating the English edition", above).

Other guards: the versions resolved stay in the uv cache, and two guards cover what changes them: `.python-version` at the root fixes the Python version, and the watch checks the uv cache every morning when uv or its Python changed (next paragraph).


### After an update of uv

The watch does this check by itself. Every morning `tools/watch.sh` compares the uv version and the Python version uv picks with those recorded in `~/.pmzilla-lab/` (`uv-version`, `uv-python-version`). When one changed (or nothing is recorded yet), it runs a throwaway script (the `# /// script` header of the wake-up script, then `import yaml`; no wake-up code is executed) from a temporary folder with the network, checks offline from a second one, writes the result in the watch log (`~/Library/Logs/pmzilla-watch.log`) and records the new versions only on success. It sends a macOS notification only on failure, and never stops the watch. The project Python is fixed by `.python-version` at the root (3.14 on 2026-10-10). The manual procedure below is for a notified failure, or to force the check (delete the two files above).

The offline wake-up test of `postures_skills` fails as long as the uv cache does not hold the `pyyaml` package for the Python version uv picks (seen on 2026-10-10 with uv 0.13.0 and Python 3.14: `Failed to download pyyaml…`, the network being cut in the sandbox). Commands with `--with` do not fill that cache. Fix it in your Terminal, with Claude Code and the watch stopped (otherwise the cache is locked): copy a wake-up script to a temporary folder, run it there once with the network, then check offline from a second temporary folder.

```bash
cd ~/PMZilla-Lab/workspace
dossier=$(mktemp -d) && cp .claude/skills/pmzilla-discovery-bmad/scripts/wake.py "$dossier"/
(cd "$dossier" && uv run wake.py)                  # with the network; the script path error it prints is of no importance
verif=$(mktemp -d) && cp .claude/skills/pmzilla-discovery-bmad/scripts/wake.py "$verif"/
(cd "$verif" && UV_OFFLINE=1 uv run wake.py)       # offline check from another folder: no download must be attempted
bash tests/run_all.sh postures_skills
```

### Mutation tests of the masking (`tools/mutation_masking.py`; change `masking-mutation-tests`)

The mutation tests check that the suites of the masking and role code (`tools/flow_views.py`, `tools/db.py`, `tools/flux_jira.py`, `tools/epic_statuses.py`, scope declared in the tool) would catch a small change of that code: a comparison inverted, a constant moved by one, `max` and `min` swapped, `in` and `not in` swapped, a condition negated, a return turned into `return None`. The in-house mutator uses the standard library only; it works on a temporary copy of the committed files (`git archive HEAD`), never on the working tree, and never reaches the database or the Keychain. Uncommitted changes are not measured: commit first.

- **On demand.** In your Terminal: `uv run tools/mutation_masking.py run` (a complete run, several minutes; `--timeout <seconds>` sets the limit per mutant). The weekly step of the daily watch runs `uv run --offline tools/mutation_masking.py run --due` by itself (Monday by default, `PMZILLA_MUTATION_DAY`; caught up when the last complete run is older than seven days); it never counts among the failed steps of the watch and never changes its exit code: a code 2 or 3 is named in the watch log (`~/Library/Logs/pmzilla-watch.log`). The suites run through `uv run --with …`, so the uv cache must have been filled once online.
- **Reading the report.** `uv run tools/mutation_masking.py report` prints the last recorded run (state file `~/.pmzilla-lab/mutation-etat.json`, owner-only, no code text): one line per target, the summary `… mutants tués sur …, score … %, seuil … %, cible … %`, then one line per survivor (`survivant <file>:<function> ligne <n> (<operator>)`) and per timed-out mutant (counted as killed). Exit codes of `run`: 0 score at or above the threshold (or not due), 2 the run cannot start (threshold, state, copy, failing reference suite), 3 score below the threshold.
- **A survivor** is fixed by a test in the suite of its module that fails on that mutant. A survivor judged equivalent (no observable behaviour changes) stays counted against the score: there is no list of accepted survivors and no exclusion.
- **Changing the threshold.** The floor (70 %) and the target (80 %, informational) are the two first non-comment lines of `tools/mutation_masking_seuil.txt` (floor from 50 to 100). The threshold changes only through a reviewed and committed change of that file, raised to the target once a run reaches it; it is never lowered to hide a survivor that a test could kill.

## 17. Doctor (`/doctor`)

The doctor is one read-only health check of the lab: `uv run tools/doctor.py` (or `/doctor` in Claude Code, which runs it and explains each check that is not ok). It prints one line per check, then a summary line. Options: `--language fr|en`, `--json` (the format of the state file), `--quiet` (only the checks that are not ok), `--record` (writes the state file and may notify; only the scheduled wrapper passes it).

| Check | What it looks at |
|---|---|
| `db` | the database host and port accept a TCP connection (local host only), and the watch run of the day did not record the database as unreachable or its tables as missing |
| `ollama`, `models` | the local Ollama answers its model listing; the embedding model and the watch-triage model named by the repository constants are present |
| `launchd` | each installed job of the repository templates (`tools/com.pmzilla.*.plist`) is loaded |
| `watch` | today's watch run ended without error (not due before 08:00). It reads the record `~/.pmzilla-lab/veille-du-jour.json` (section 8), never the log, and writes nothing, takes no lock and writes no alert marker. Reasons beyond `ok`, `failed`, `running`, `missing` and `not_due`: `interrupted` (state `interrompu`, or `commence` while `veille.verrou` does not exist), `postponed` (state `reporte` before 22:00, skipped), `missed` (after the latest start time, 22:00 by default, no record of today or a record that says `reporte`: problem) and `record_not_writable` (the data folder is not writable, or the alert marker `pmzilla-watch-alerte` holds today: problem) |
| `backup` | today's backup folder holds one dump per schema plus the partial dump of `quality` (by file name and count only; not due before 08:30) |
| `offsite` | age of the last successful off-site copy, from the copy line of the off-site status file (warning after 1 day without a successful copy, problem after 15), and the last 40 lines of the copy log `~/Library/Logs/pmzilla-offsite-copy.log`: a problem (`not_permitted` for "Operation not permitted", `incomplete` for "copie incomplète") when they hold one of these phrases and the log was written after the last success. It prints no log text and no path |
| `disk` | free space of the volume that holds the home folder (warning under 20 GB, problem under 5 GB) |
| `jira` | the token expiry date, see below |
| `branches` | worktrees and branches merged into `main` and not deleted, worktrees inactive for more than 14 days (last commit date), prunable worktrees. Warning only |
| `run-worktree` | the worktree the scheduled jobs run from is on `main` |

Levels: `ok`, `warning`, `problem`, `skipped` (not applicable now, with its reason). Exit codes: 0 when no check is a problem (warnings and skipped included), 1 when at least one is, 2 on a usage error or when the doctor cannot run (for example, the state file cannot be written with `--record`). Every threshold and due time is a constant of the script, overridable by an environment variable listed in the docstring of `tools/doctor.py`.

**Jira token expiry.** The Jira API token has an expiry date that Jira does not expose. When you create the token, write its non-secret expiry date in `Flux-Lab/flux.yaml`, under `jira_token_expires` (the key added by the Jira alignment change, R-58), for example `jira_token_expires: 2027-03-31`. The doctor reads only that date: warning within the warning period, problem once it has passed, `skipped` with "expiry date unknown" while the key is absent. It never reads the Keychain nor the token.

**Scheduled run and dashboard.** The job `com.pmzilla.doctor` (template `tools/com.pmzilla.doctor.plist`) runs `tools/doctor.sh` every day at 09:00, after the watch, the backup and the off-site copy. The wrapper runs `doctor.py --record` with `DOCTOR_NOTIFIER=1`, then regenerates the dashboard without ever failing on it, and exits with the doctor's code. The install script installs and loads this job with the other jobs, even when the watch and backup jobs are deferred (the doctor needs neither Ollama nor the container runtime). `--record` writes `~/.pmzilla-lab/doctor-etat.json` (owner read and write only, replaced atomically; counts and reason keys, never a path, a branch name, a model name or a secret). The first section of the dashboard, "Health", reads only that file. One macOS notification is sent when at least one check is a problem, at most once a day, never for a warning and never on a manual run.

**What the doctor never does.** It starts, loads, unloads, pulls, deletes and prunes nothing. It opens no database session, reads no Keychain entry, opens no file of the backups folder, mounts no off-site disk, runs no watch or backup, contacts only the local machine and compares no version (versions belong to the components watch, R-41). `tools/doctor.sh` regenerates the dashboard, which opens a database session: Claude Code never runs it (deny rules); it runs `doctor.py`.

## 18. Components watch

Every component the lab depends on is listed in `docs/pmzilla/composants.yaml`, with where its version is fixed and the heading below that describes its update. Each update you retain becomes a job of the work queue, run with a pinned target version; nothing below runs by itself.

### Components watch

- **Inventory.** One entry per component: upstream name, kind (`python-package`, `python-runtime`, `node-runtime`, `tool`, `docker-image`, `ollama-model`, `bmad-module`, `vendored`), where its version is fixed (`pinned_in`), the channel of an unpinned tool, the type of source of new versions (`upstream`), the heading of its update procedure (`procedure`), and optional flags (`requires-reindex` for the embedding model, `major-watch-only` for the database image). The version itself is never copied there: it is read where it is fixed. `uv run tools/composants.py inventory` prints the version used of each component, offline; `--verify` also rebuilds the list from the repository sources (script headers and lock files, `.python-version`, `mothra/.node-version`, `docker-compose.yml`, the two model settings, the install prerequisites, the BMAD manifest, `openspec/config.yaml`, `tools/vendor/`, the extra dependencies of `tests/run_all.sh`). The suite `tests/composants` runs it: a commit that adds a component without its entry fails and names what to add or remove, and a tag of the database image changed without the `Version` and `Digest` lines of its comment fails too.
- **Check.** `uv run tools/composants.py check` (in your Terminal; Claude Code never runs it) compares each component with the latest published version: updates, major versions, pre-releases and republished tags (an Ollama model or the image republished under the same tag) are reported apart, with one `Job proposé : Mettre à jour <name> <used> → <available>` line each, and a dry run `uv lock --script --upgrade` on temporary copies counts the packages each script lock would move. Options: `--only <name>` (one component, state unchanged), `--timeout <seconds>` (5 by default). A source that does not answer makes the check incomplete (`Veille des composants incomplète`), never a failure. Exit codes: 0 nothing to notify, 1 something to notify, 2 the check cannot run.
- **What the check never does.** It installs, upgrades, pulls and starts nothing (no `brew update`, no model download, no image pull, no `uv sync`), never cleans the uv cache, writes no file of the repository, sends no credential and retries no request.
- **State.** `~/.pmzilla-lab/composants-etat.json` (owner only, replaced atomically): dates of the last attempt and of the last complete check, the result per component and the versions already notified. The last section of the dashboard, "Composants", reads only this file.
- **Weekly run.** Every morning `tools/watch.sh` runs `check --if-due` just before the dashboard, whatever happened to the database and Ollama. The check is due once a week: on Monday (`PMZILLA_COMPOSANTS_DAY`), or at the first following run when the last complete check is more than seven days old. One macOS notification comes for a version not yet notified, and, on the weekly day, when no complete check happened for more than three weeks (`PMZILLA_COMPOSANTS_STALE_WEEKS`). The step never counts as a failed step of the watch.
- **From a novelty to a job.** Read the novelty and its procedure below, then add a `direct-prompt` job to the work queue from the suggested title, with the target version pinned (model: R-39). Nothing writes the work queue for you.

### Update a Python package of the scripts

Versions live in the lock files `tools/<script>.py.lock` ("Script dependencies", section 16). In a branch: `uv lock --script tools/<script>.py --upgrade-package <package>` for each script whose lock holds the package (`uv run tools/composants.py inventory` lists them), then `bash tests/run_all.sh`, and commit the locks together. A major version is a job of its own, read with the package's release notes.

### Update the Python runtime

`.python-version` at the root fixes the Python of every script. Change it in a branch, run `uv python install <version>` in your Terminal, regenerate every lock (`uv lock --script tools/<script>.py` for each lock), run the full suite, then follow "After an update of uv" (section 16): the uv cache of the scripts must be filled again for the new Python.

### Update the database image

The `image:` line of `docker-compose.yml` and the `Version` and `Digest` lines of its header comment move together (the inventory test fails otherwise). Pull and test the new tag yourself (`docker pull`, then the suites with a throwaway database, section 14.5, and the library parity), take a backup (section 14.2), then update the tag and the digest in one commit. A higher PostgreSQL line (`-pg<N>`) changes the data folder format: it is a migration, never a simple update.

After the tag is updated, the extension of the existing database must follow, because the data volume keeps the old extension version (`CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS` does not update it). In your Terminal, as superuser, connected to the library database:

1. Before the update, copy the data volume: it is the way back. The per-schema dumps (section 14.2) do not carry the roles or their passwords, so they alone do not restore a working database.
2. After `bash tools/db_up.sh` has recreated the container with the new image: `ALTER EXTENSION pg_search UPDATE TO '<version>';` (for example `'0.26.2'`).
3. Check that `extversion` of `pg_extension` (extension `pg_search`) equals `select paradedb.version_info();`. If they differ, restart the database (`bash tools/db_up.sh down`, then `bash tools/db_up.sh`). The doctor does not make this check: it opens no database session and compares no version.
4. As a precaution, rebuild the three BM25 indexes (`SET max_parallel_maintenance_workers = 0;` then `REINDEX INDEX knowledge.chunks_bm25;`, `REINDEX INDEX cases.cases_bm25;`, `REINDEX INDEX cases.experiments_bm25;`), then run the checks of section 14.6 (probe and library parity). The restore test of the backups (`tools/db_restore_test.sh`) prints the rebuild time of each index in a throwaway container: it is an estimate of this step.

### Update an Ollama model

Models are fetched by `ollama pull <model>` in your Terminal, never by a tool of the lab. A republished `bge-m3` tag changes the search vectors: after the pull, reindex the whole library (section 5) and run the library parity before relying on search again. For the pre-triage model, run the watch calibration again after the pull.

### Update BMAD

Run `npx bmad-method install` again at the target version (section 4, "Updating BMAD"), replay the text replacements as described there, then commit `_bmad/` and the skills it rewrote. External modules (`bmb`) are updated by the same installer.

### Update the vendored chart library

Run `zsh tools/vendor/telecharger_echarts.sh <version>` (section 10, "Visual flow dashboard"): it checks the registry integrity and rewrites `echarts.min.js`, `echarts.sha256` and `echarts.version`. Generate the flow dashboard once, then commit the five files.

### Update a Homebrew tool

`brew upgrade <formula>` (or `brew upgrade --cask ollama-app`: Homebrew renamed the Ollama cask) in your Terminal, outside a Claude Code session, then `bash tests/run_all.sh`. Homebrew itself updates with `brew update`. For colima and docker, stop the database first (`docker compose down`, the volume is kept) and start it again with `bash tools/db_up.sh`.

### Update git (Xcode Command Line Tools)

On the author's Mac, git is Apple's (`/usr/bin/git`, "Apple Git"), installed with the Xcode Command Line Tools. Apple ships it on its own schedule, behind the upstream git releases, so the components watch only records the local version and never reports a novelty for it. It is updated by the macOS software update (System Settings, General, Software Update, or `softwareupdate --list` then `softwareupdate --install <label>` in your Terminal). A Mac where git came from Homebrew follows "Update a Homebrew tool" instead; change its inventory entry accordingly.

### Update Node.js (fnm)

On the author's Mac, node is managed by fnm, not Homebrew; the components watch compares it with the Node.js releases. In your Terminal: `fnm install <version> && fnm default <version>`, open a new terminal, check `node --version`, then run `npx bmad-method --version` (BMAD's installer runs on node). A Mac where node came from Homebrew follows "Update a Homebrew tool" instead. A new node brings a new npm: rerun the alias inventory of "Mothra sessions: npm, npx and node".

### Update the Node of Mothra

Three Node entries exist and must not be mixed up. `node` is the Node that runs BMAD's installer: not pinned, followed through fnm (previous heading). `node-mothra` (kind `node-runtime`) is the Node of Mothra, pinned in one file, `mothra/.node-version`, and its watch reports only LTS lines as major versions: a line still marked "Current" is never reported. `library/node` is the base image of the local CI (`ci/Dockerfile`, "Update the CI image"): its tag equals the version of `mothra/.node-version` and moves only through this procedure. This procedure serves the first installation and every move to a new line.

1. Read the official Node release calendar. Continue only if the target line is "Active LTS", not "Current".
2. In your Terminal, install the latest patch of that line with the version manager (`fnm install <line>`), then run `node --version` in `mothra/`: it gives the exact version.
3. Write that version in `mothra/.node-version`: one line, three numeric segments (`MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH`), no `v` prefix, range or alias such as `lts/*`. In the same commit, change the base line of `ci/Dockerfile` (the local CI image) and, once it exists, `engines` in `mothra/package.json`; the image tag is the exact version of the file, and its `sha256` digest changes with it (read the digest of the new tag in your Terminal, for example with `docker buildx imagetools inspect node:<tag>`): the tag holds the version, the digest fixes the image that runs.
4. Run `uv run tools/composants.py inventory --verify`, the `composants` suite, the npm suite once it exists, then the full runner, and `bash tools/ci_local.sh up <branche>` (the image key changes, so `up` rebuilds the image). An invalid version file (the message quotes only its first line, cut at 40 characters), a Node of the CI image that differs from the file's, or a file without its inventory entry (or the reverse) fails there. The image check reads the tag of the `library/node` base in its `node:`, `library/node:`, `docker.io/library/node:` or `index.docker.io/library/node:` forms; it is skipped, with its reason, when `ci/Dockerfile` or the version file does not exist.
5. Moving to the next LTS line is a planned item of the work queue, created when the weekly check reports it as a major version.

### Update Claude Code

Claude Code updates itself through its official installer; to force it, run `claude update` in your Terminal, with no session open. Check `claude --version`, then open a session and check that the session-start hook prints its line.

### Update the OpenSpec CLI

`npm install -g @fission-ai/openspec@<version>` in your Terminal, then `openspec validate --specs` on the repository: the specs must stay valid with the new CLI.

### Update the CI image

The local CI image (`ci/Dockerfile`) pins its base (official Node slim image), the uv image and the OpenSpec CLI; their inventory entries say `pinned_in: CI Dockerfile` and the values live in `ci/Dockerfile` only. The Node base is the exception: its tag equals the version of `mothra/.node-version` and moves only through "Update the Node of Mothra", which changes the tag and its digest together; this procedure changes the uv image, the OpenSpec CLI, or the digest of an unchanged Node tag. In a branch, change the tag and its `sha256` digest together (read the digest of the multi-architecture index in your Terminal, for example with `docker buildx imagetools inspect <image>:<tag>`), or the exact CLI version, then run `bash tests/run_all.sh composants` and `bash tools/ci_local.sh up <branche>`: the key changes, so `up` rebuilds the image before the run. A value marked `A_REMPLIR_PAR_L_AUTEUR` is one you still have to read; `tests/composants` names it until it is filled.
